. This condition requires the following (in the notation for the
p
case, the
q
case being entirely symmetric):

(a)

f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}
is continuous in
(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w_{p})
for all parameters
(\gamma^{\mathrm{in}},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}})
.

(b)

In a neighborhood of
\left(\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)
,
f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}
is Lipschitz in
(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}})
for all
w_{p}
, with Lipschitz constant continuous in
w_{p}
.

(c)

The domain of
w_{p}
is compact.

(d)

The Lipschitz uniformity over the parameters is satisfied for all inputs, i.e.

\left|f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}\left(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w_{p},\gamma_{1}\right)-f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}\left(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w_{p},\gamma_{2}\right)\right|\leq L\left(1+\|\left(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w_{p}\right)\|\right)\left|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}\right|

for all
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
in a neighborhood of
\overline{\gamma}_{pk}
.

It is easy to see that the
f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}
given in the translation (
4.2
) are separable by definition and either uniformly Lipschitz or UBCL. In particular, the only functions which are not simply uniformly Lipschitz are the
[f_{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}]_{2}=[w_{q}]^{1}q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
. However, it is immediately clear that these are UBCL since they are free of
\gamma_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and since
w_{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
consists of the singular values which were assumed to lie in a compact interval
[0,S_{\max}]
in
Assumption 0
. We note that the
f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}
are separable and uniformly Lipschitz when the
g_{x1}/g_{z1}
are, which is guaranteed by
Assumption 3
above.

Condition 4.
The functions
C^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p},C^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q},\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p},\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}
are bounded over their domains and Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant independent of
N
and
k
.
For the functions given in the translation (
4.2
), this condition is satisfied as long as the domains of the functions are compact (and independent of
N
and
k
). We note that clipping of the
\gamma_{1}
and
\alpha_{1}
scalars is guaranteed by (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) along with
Assumption 4
.

Condition 5.
Following
[
38
]
, we define stopping criteria that determine when the algorithm has effectively converged. First, we stop the iteration if
\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}<\epsilon_{1}
or
\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}<\epsilon_{2}
. In terms Algorithm
1
, this is equivalent to stopping the algorithm when the variance of
|R_{1k}-X_{0}|
or
|R_{2k}-X_{0}|
is sufficiently small. Next, we stop if
\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}<\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}
or
\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}<\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}
, where these
\rho
quantities are defined in the next section. This condition essentially stops the algorithm if the difference in the (asymptotic distributions of) successive iterates is sufficiently small.

Condition 6.
The interval
[t_{\min},t_{\max}]
within which the
\alpha_{1}
scalars are truncated includes the
[\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{1}
and
[\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}]_{1}
(defined in the general recursion state evolution (
4.36
)) for all iterations
k
prior to termination.

This condition is satisfiable as long as these
[\overline{\alpha}]_{1}
scalars do not converge to
1
or
0
as
k\to\infty
. But as long as the second derivatives in (
3.1
) are bounded away from zero over any compact subset of their domains, then for either the MAP or MMSE denoisers
g_{x1}
and
g_{z1}
, the derivatives of the
f_{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
f_{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
will be bounded away from
1
and
0
over any compact subset of their domains (see e.g. Lemma 1 in
[
37
]
). Likewise, if the asymptotic variances
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
and
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
(also defined in (
4.36
)) do not diverge or shrink to
0
before termination, the
[\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{1}
and
[\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}]_{1}
cannot converge to
1
or
0
as
k\to\infty
because the
\gamma
scalars are clipped to a compact interval.

4.2
Notation

In the above translation between the general recursion and GVAMP, we used general iterates of dimension
d=2
in order to track the GVAMP iterates, the initial data, and the transformed input
\mathbf{z}_{0}
throughout the algorithm. Henceforth, we will analyze the general recursion with
d=1
in order to simplify notation and proof ideas. However, these arguments can all be extended to the case of general dimension
d
(see Appendix
9
for details on how the following results generalize to
d>1
). For
k\geq 0
, define matrices
\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(k+1)}
having columns
\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq k
, and define
N\times(k+1)
matrices
\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
,
\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
, and
\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
analogously.

Now, for
k\geq 1
, define
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk},\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(2k+1)}
to be the matrices containing, respectively, the
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
iterates of the algorithm up to and including
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
and the
\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
iterates up to and including
\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
. Similarly, let
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk},\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times 2k}
be the matrices containing, respectively, the
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
iterates up to and including
\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}
and the
\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
iterates up to and including
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}
. We use the initial conditions
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}=\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}=\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{u0}=\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}=\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},
and
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{v0}=\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q0}=\emptyset
.
In other words, these matrices are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}=[\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\;\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}],\;

\displaystyle\quad\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}=[\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\;\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}],

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}=[\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}\;\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}],\;

\displaystyle\quad\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}=[\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}\;\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}],

(4.6)

where for two matrices
\mathbf{M}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times p_{1}}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times p_{2}}
, by the notation
\mathbf{M}=[\mathbf{M}_{1}\;\mathbf{M}_{2}]
, we mean the
N\times(p_{1}+p_{2})
matrix obtained from concatenating the columns of
\mathbf{M}_{1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}
.

Next, we define sigma-algebras generated by previous algorithm output at any iteration using the matrices defined in the previous paragraph. We use the initializations
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}=\sigma\{\mathbf{w}_{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}\}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}=\sigma\{\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}\}
, and for
k\geq 1
define

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}&=\sigma\{\mathbf{w}_{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k},\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1},\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}\},\\
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}&=\sigma\{\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k},\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k},\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}\}.\end{split}

(4.7)

Notice that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
includes the iterates of the algorithm up to and including
\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
(i.e. all output after completing the
(k-1)^{th}
iteration of the algorithm) and
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
includes the iterates up to and including
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
(i.e. all output through step (
6
) in the
k^{th}
iteration). We define the combined sigma-algebras
\mathcal{P}_{k}=\sigma\left\{\mathcal{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right\}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{k}=\sigma\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{Q}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right\}
.

Finally, for
k\geq 0
, define the matrices
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}},\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(N-2k)}
, and
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}},\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(N-2k-1)}
where, for example,
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}}
has columns that form an orthonormal basis for
\mathrm{span}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})^{\perp}
where
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
is defined in (
4.2
), and the other matrices are defined analogously.
Moreover, for
k\geq 1
, define the matrix
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times 2k}
to have as columns an orthonormal basis for
\mathrm{span}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk})
, and for
k\geq 0
, define
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(2k+1)}
as a matrix having as columns an orthonormal basis for
\mathrm{span}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk})
.
Then we let
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}=\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be any (deterministic) orthogonal matrix, and for
k\geq 1
, we define the orthogonal matrices

\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}=[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\;\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}]\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q(k-1)}=[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u(k-1)}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\;\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u(k-1)}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}]\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}.

(4.8)

To wrap up the notation, we state a definition and lemma that will be useful throughout the proof.

Definition 6

(Isotropic Invariance)
.

A vector
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
is
isotropically invariant
(or, spherically symmetric) if
\mathbf{V}_{0}\mathbf{x}\overset{d}{=}\mathbf{x}
for any orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{V}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
. Notice this implies that
\mathbf{x}/\|\mathbf{x}\|
is uniformly distributed on the unit sphere.

The following lemma about isotropically invariant vectors will be used throughout the proof.

Lemma 1
.

\mathbf{x}\overset{d}{=}\|\mathbf{x}\|(\mathbf{w}_{0}/\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|)
where
\mathbf{w}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\mathbb{I}_{N\times N})
for any isotropically invariant
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
.

Proof.

The result follows from two facts. First,
\mathbf{x}/\|\mathbf{x}\|\overset{d}{=}\mathbf{w}_{0}/\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|
since both
\mathbf{x}/\|\mathbf{x}\|
and
\mathbf{w}_{0}/\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|
are uniformly distributed on the unit sphere. Second,
\mathbf{x}/\|\mathbf{x}\|
and
\|\mathbf{x}\|
are independent. To see this, notice that
\mathbf{x}/\|\mathbf{x}\|
is uniformly-distributed on the sphere irrespective of the value of
\|\mathbf{x}\|
; thus, they are independent. Therefore,
\mathbf{x}=\|\mathbf{x}\|(\mathbf{x}/\|\mathbf{x}\|)\overset{d}{=}\|\mathbf{x}\|(\mathbf{w}_{0}/\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|)
, giving the desired result.
∎

4.3
General Algorithm Concentration

Under the conditions given in Section
4.1
, we state our general concentration result for Algorithm
3
in Lemmas
3
-
5
.
To obtain our concentration results, we first introduce deviance terms
\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
and
\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
quantifying the discrepancy between the finite sample behavior of the iterates
(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{1},...,\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k})
and
(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{1},...,\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k})
and their limiting values. This is done in Lemma
3
by studying the distributions of the vectors conditional on the previous output of the algorithm, summarized by the sigma-algebras in (
4.7
). Before we state and prove Lemma
3
, we state a lemma, originally from
[
36
, Lemmas 4 and 5]
, that characterizes the distribution of random Haar matrices, conditional on linear constraints.

Lemma 2
.

[
36
, Lemmas 4 and 5]

Let
\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
be a random, Haar-distributed matrix (see Definition
4
). Suppose that for deterministic matrices
\textbf{M}_{1},\textbf{M}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times s}
, for some
1\leq s\leq N
, we know that
\mathbf{V}
satisfies
\textbf{M}_{1}=\mathbf{V}\textbf{M}_{2}
. Then, if
\textbf{M}_{1}
and
\textbf{M}_{2}
are full column rank,

\mathbf{V}\Big\lvert_{\{\textbf{M}_{1}=\mathbf{V}\textbf{M}_{2}\}}\overset{d}{=}\textbf{M}_{1}(\textbf{M}_{1}^{T}\textbf{M}_{1})^{-1}\textbf{M}_{2}^{T}+\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\textbf{M}_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\textbf{M}_{2}}]^{T},

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}
is Haar distributed, independent of
\mathbf{V}
, and
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\textbf{M}_{1}},\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\textbf{M}_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(N-s)}
are any matrices whose columns are orthogonal bases for
Range(\mathbf{M}_{1})^{\perp}
and
Range(\mathbf{M}_{2})^{\perp}
, respectively.

To use the above lemma, we notice that conditioning on sigma-algebras
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
, defined in (
4.7
), is equivalent to conditioning on two linear constraints. As an illustrative example, consider conditioning on the sigma-algebra
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\sigma\{\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k-1}\}
, which contains all of the input iterates up until just before step (
9
). First, notice that the action of
\mathbf{V}
in Algorithm
3
is only in step (
4
), which reads
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\leftarrow\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
, and step (
9
), which reads
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\leftarrow\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
. Thus, conditioning on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
is equivalent to conditioning on the linear constraint
[\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\;\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k-1}]=\mathbf{V}[\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\;\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k-1}]
or, equivalently, on
\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}
for
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\mathbf{C}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}}
defined in (
4.2
).
By a similar argument, we can see that conditioning on
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1}=\sigma\{\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1},\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\}
is equivalent to conditioning on
[\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\;\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]=\mathbf{V}[\mathbf{U}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\;\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]
or on
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k+1)}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q(k+1)}
. Continuing this argument, conditioning on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
or
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k+1}
is equivalent to conditioning on
\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{uk}
or
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{v(k+1)}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q(k+1)}
, respectively.

Now we give the conditional distribution lemma, Lemma
3
. In the lemma and in what follows, we use the notation
\mathbf{0}_{k}
to denotes a vector of zeros of length
k
. This lemma and the following, Lemma
4
, depend on vectors
\bm{\beta}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\bm{\beta}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and constants
\rho_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\rho_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, which we leave unspecified for the time being. The statements of these lemmas are valid for any choice of these parameters, and in section
4.5
we define them as appropriate limits of GVAMP quantities so as to ensure the desired concentration behavior.

Lemma 3
.

If
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}
has full rank for
0\leq k\leq K
, then we have for all such
k
that

\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\lvert_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0},\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{\ell+1}\,\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},

(4.9)

where
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is defined in (
4.8
) while
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
are unspecified for the time being, with appropriate values given in what follows in (
4.41
), and

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\right)\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0},

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\left(([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\\
\bm{0}_{k}\end{matrix}\right]\right)+\left[\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}.

The matrices
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}
and
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
are defined in (
4.2
). In the above,
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
are length-
N
vectors with independent, standard Gaussian entries that are independent across
0\leq k\leq K
and independent of the corresponding conditioning sigma-algebra
\mathcal{P}_{k}
. These are decomposed as
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\lvert\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]
where
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is length
N-2k
and
\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is length
2k
. In particular, this means
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}+\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
.

If
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
has full rank for
0\leq k\leq K
, we have for all such
k
that

\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0},\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]_{\ell+1}\,\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk},

(4.10)

where
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
is defined in (
4.8
) while
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
and
\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
are unspecified for the time being, with appropriate values given in what follows in (
4.41
), and

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}

\displaystyle=\frac{[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}}{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}+\left[\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\,\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\left(([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}_{k+1}\\
\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\end{matrix}\right]\right)+\left[\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}}\,\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}.

(4.12)

The matrices
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}
and
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
are defined in (
4.2
). In the above,
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
are length-
N
vectors with independent, standard Gaussian entries that are independent across
0\leq k\leq K
and independent of the corresponding conditioning sigma-algebra
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
. As above, these are decomposed as
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}=[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\,\lvert\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]
where
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
is length
N-2k-1
and
\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
is length
2k+1
. Again we have that
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}=\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}+\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
. The same statement holds with all input variables replaced by their output equivalents.

In both cases, the output statements for
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}
and
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}
are analogous to those given in (
9.2
) and (
4.10
) with
\mathrm{out}
replacing
\mathrm{in}
everywhere.

Furthermore, we have that
\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{P}_{k}
and that
\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
for all
k\geq 0
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
and
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
, as the base case for
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
is slightly more involved than that of
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
. The proof for
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
and
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
is broadly similar, and the proof for the output variables exactly mirrors the proof for the input variables.

We first prove the statement for
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
. Observe from the Algorithm
3
definition,
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}=\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
and that
\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}
since
\mathcal{Q}_{0}=\sigma\left\{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}\right\}=\sigma\{\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}\}
, as defined in (
4.7
).

By the reasoning in the paragraph just before the lemma statement, conditioning on
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
is equivalent to conditioning on the linear constraints
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}
and
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{uk}
. Thus,

\mathbf{V}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}=\mathbf{V}\lvert_{\left\{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk},\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{uk}\right\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\mathbf{V}\lvert_{\left\{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\right\}}=\mathbf{V}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}},

where
(a)
follows as
\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{V}
are assumed to be independent.
The right-hand side can be handled by Lemma
2
. In particular, the conditional distribution of
\mathbf{V}
given
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
can be decomposed as

\mathbf{V}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]^{T}+\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T},

(4.13)

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}
is a random
(N-(2k+1))\times(N-(2k+1))
matrix that is independent of
\mathbf{V}
, but taking the same distribution, in that it is Haar (or uniformly) distributed on the group of orthogonal matrices of its dimensions.
In what follows, we drop the explicit “
\mathrm{in}
” superscript to save space because throughout we will be working with the input version of the variables.

Thus, for
k=0
, conditioning on
\mathcal{Q}_{0}=\sigma\{\mathbf{w}_{q},\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{p}_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0}\}
is equivalent to conditioning on
\mathbf{C}_{p0}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{C}_{u0}
(since
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{w}_{q}
are independent), and the result in (
4.13
) gives

\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{C}_{u0}(\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{T}\mathbf{C}_{p0})^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}+\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0},

(4.14)

where we have removed the “
\mathrm{in}
” superscript from the vector
\mathbf{v}_{0}
and the matrices
\mathbf{C}_{u0}
and
\mathbf{C}_{p0}
.
Now observe that
\mathbf{C}_{p0}=\mathbf{p}_{0}
and
\mathbf{C}_{u0}=\mathbf{u}_{0}
, by definition in (
4.2
), and therefore,

\mathbf{C}_{u0}(\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{T}\mathbf{C}_{p0})^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}=\mathbf{u}_{0}{\|\mathbf{p}_{0}\|^{-2}}\mathbf{p}_{0}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}.

(4.15)

Next, as
\mathbf{p}_{0}
and
\mathbf{v}_{0}
are both measurable with respect to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
, so is the vector
[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}
. We will next argue that the vector
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}
is isotropically invariant as defined in Definition
6
. To see this, recall that
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}
is Haar-distributed (see Definition
4
), thus
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\overset{d}{=}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}
for any other orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1\times N-1}
. Hence,
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\overset{d}{=}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}
, meaning
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}
is isotropically invariant; therefore, by Lemma
1
,
its distribution is entirely determined by the distribution of its magnitude. In other words, since
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|=\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|
, we have

\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|\left({\mathbf{Z}_{q0}}/{\|\mathbf{Z}_{q0}\|}\right),

(4.16)

for
\mathbf{Z}_{q0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\mathbb{I}_{N-1\times N-1})
, independent of
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
. This gives us that, conditional on
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}_{q0}\|}\,\,\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\mathbf{Z}_{q0}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}_{q0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho_{q0}}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\,\mathbf{Z}_{q0}-\sqrt{\rho_{q0}}\,\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}+\sqrt{\rho_{q0}}\,\mathbf{O}_{q0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}.

(4.17)

The second equality in the above uses that
\mathbf{O}_{q0}=[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\;\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}]\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
by (
4.8
) for
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times 1}
and
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(N-1)}
; therefore,
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\mathbf{Z}_{q0}=\mathbf{O}_{q0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}-\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}
.

Plugging result (
4.15
) and (
4.17
) into (
4.14
), we find the result in (
4.10
)-(
4.11
), namely

\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{p}_{0}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}}{\|\mathbf{p}_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\mathbf{u}_{0}+\left[\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}_{q0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho_{q0}}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\,\mathbf{Z}_{q0}-\sqrt{\rho_{q0}}\,\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}}\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}+\sqrt{\rho_{q0}}\,\mathbf{O}_{q0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}.

To establish the statement for
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
, notice that
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
by Algorithm
3
, so that
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}=\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}
(where
\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in\mathcal{Q}_{k}
). Recall from the discussion for the
k=0
case above that
\mathbf{V}^{T}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{V}^{T}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}}
. Thus, as before, we drop the explicit “
\mathrm{in}
” superscript, and we can use Lemma
2
to write

\begin{split}\mathbf{q}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{C}_{uk}(\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{T}\mathbf{C}_{pk})^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k}+\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k}\\
&=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{qk}]_{\ell+1}\mathbf{q}_{\ell}+\mathbf{C}_{uk}\left((\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{T}\mathbf{C}_{pk})^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k}-\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}_{k+1}\\
\bm{\beta}_{qk}\end{matrix}\right]\right)+\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k},\end{split}

(4.18)

where we recall that
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}
is Haar-distributed and independent of the conditioning sigma-algebra. The second equality in (
4.18
) follows from the fact that
\mathbf{C}_{uk}=[\mathbf{U}_{k}\;\mathbf{Q}_{k-1}]
from (
4.2
); thus,

\mathbf{C}_{uk}\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}_{k+1}\\
\bm{\beta}_{qk}\end{matrix}\right]=\mathbf{U}_{k}\mathbf{0}_{k+1}+\mathbf{Q}_{k-1}\bm{\beta}_{qk}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{qk}]_{\ell+1}\mathbf{q}_{\ell}.

As above in (
4.16
)-(
4.17
), because
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}
is Haar distributed and because
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\mathbf{Z}_{qk}=\mathbf{O}_{qk}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}-\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}
, we find that conditional on
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}_{qk}\|}\,\,\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\,\mathbf{Z}_{qk}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}_{qk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho_{qk}}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\,\mathbf{Z}_{qk}-\sqrt{\rho_{qk}}\,\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}+\sqrt{\rho_{qk}}\,\mathbf{O}_{qk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}.

Combining with (
4.18
) and using the definition of
\bm{\Delta}_{qk}
in (
4.12
), we find the desired result:

\displaystyle\mathbf{q}_{k}\lvert_{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{qk}]_{\ell+1}\mathbf{q}_{\ell}+\mathbf{C}_{uk}\left((\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{T}\mathbf{C}_{pk})^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{k}-\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}_{k+1}\\
\bm{\beta}_{qk}\end{matrix}\right]\right)+\sqrt{\rho_{qk}}\,\mathbf{O}_{qk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\sqrt{\rho_{qk}}\,\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\,\breve{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}+\left[\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}_{qk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho_{qk}}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}}\mathbf{Z}_{qk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{qk}]_{\ell+1}\mathbf{q}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho_{qk}}\,\mathbf{O}_{qk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}+\bm{\Delta}_{qk}.

Finally, we establish the conditional independence of
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
given the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
for all
k\geq 0
. Notice that conditioning on
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
is equivalent to conditioning on
\left\{g(\mathbf{V})=0,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right\}
where
g,h
are the (deterministic) functions
\mathbf{X}\mapsto\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}
and
\mathbf{X}\mapsto\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{out}}_{uk}
, respectively. This is just a reformulation of the linear constraints discussed earlier. Hence,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A,\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid\mathcal{Q}_{k}\right)&=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A,\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid g(\mathbf{V})=0,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A,\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid g(\mathbf{V})=0,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right).\end{split}

(4.19)

In the above, the second equality follows because
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}=\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
by Algorithm
3
step (
9
). Now applying the definition of conditional probability,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A,\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid\mathcal{Q}_{k}\right)&=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A,\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B,g(\mathbf{V})=0,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left(g(\mathbf{V})=0,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)}\\
&=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A,g(\mathbf{V})=0\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left(g(\mathbf{V})=0\right)}\times\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left(h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)}\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A\mid g(\mathbf{V})=0\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid h(\mathbf{U})=0\right).\end{split}

(4.20)

The second equality of (
4.20
) follows as
\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{V}
are independent and
\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
(and because conditioning on
\left\{g(\mathbf{V})=0\right\}
or
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
is equivalent; similarly for
\left\{h(\mathbf{U})=0\right\}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
).
This establishes conditional independence of
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
given
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A\mid g(\mathbf{V})=0\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A\mid g(\mathbf{V})=0,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid g(\mathbf{V})=0,h(\mathbf{U})=0\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\in A\mid\mathcal{Q}_{k}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\in B\mid\mathcal{Q}_{k}\right).

∎

Next we extend this lemma to a result characterizing the discrepancy between the finite sample iterates and the limiting behavior of the
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{q}
iterates jointly. To do this, we introduce a new recursion in terms of Gaussian vectors,
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}
and
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}
defined in Lemma
3
. As we see in Lemma
4
, the iterates generated by this recursion, referred to as the equivalent Gaussian recursion, are jointly equal in distribution to the iterates in the general recursion, Algorithm
3
.

Algorithm 5

Equivalent Gaussian Representation of General Recursion

1:

Separable denoisers
\mathbf{f}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}
,
\mathbf{f}_{p}^{\mathrm{out}}
,
\mathbf{f}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}
, and
\mathbf{f}_{q}^{\mathrm{out}}
;
parameter update functions
\Gamma_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
; initial data
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
; and disturbance vectors
\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
.

2:

\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\leftarrow\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{r}\,\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\right),

\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\leftarrow\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{r}\,\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{pr}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{out}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pr}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pr}\right),

3:

\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\left[\mathbf{f}_{p}^{\mathrm{in}}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)-\alpha_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}\right]
,

\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\left[\mathbf{f}_{p}^{\mathrm{out}}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)-\alpha_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right],

4:

\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\leftarrow\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{r}\,\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{qr}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{qr}^{\mathrm{in}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qr}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qr}\right),

\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\leftarrow\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}_{qk}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{r}\,\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{qr}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{qr}^{\mathrm{out}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qr}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qr}\right),

5:

\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{in}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}}\left[\mathbf{f}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q},\gamma_{qk}^{\mathrm{out}},\gamma_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)-\alpha_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}\right]
,

\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{out}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}}\left[\mathbf{f}_{q}^{\mathrm{out}}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q},\gamma_{qk}^{\mathrm{out}},\gamma_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)-\alpha_{qk}^{\mathrm{out}}\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right].

In the above, all relevant scalar quantities (e.g.
\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
) are understood to be defined analogously as in the general recursion, Algorithm
3
, but now in terms of the Gaussian equivalent iterates (e.g.
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
). Likewise, the discrepancy terms
\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
are defined as those in Lemma
3
, but using the Gaussian equivalent iterates. Constant vectors
\mathbf{c}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
\mathbf{c}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
are defined explicitly (
9.5
) below in terms of the
\bm{\beta}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
\bm{\beta}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
vectors. These vectors, along with
\rho_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
\rho_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
remain unspecified, as the statement of Lemma
4
is valid for any choice of these constants.

The Gaussian equivalent recursion is initialized in the same way as the GVAMP recursion, so in particular
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}=\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}
, and thus
\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}=\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
. Furthermore,
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
are deterministic orthogonal matrices, so we choose them to be equal as well.

In order to state Lemma
4
, we first introduce some new notation. For all
j\geq 0
, we will let

\mathbf{r}_{j}=\left(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j}\right),\hskip 28.45274pt\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}_{j}=\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j}\right),

(4.21)

where the terms in
\mathbf{r}_{j}
are those in Algorithm
3
, while those in
\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}_{j}
are from Algorithm
5
.
We also define the concatenation of these up through iteration
k
as

\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k}=\left(\mathbf{r}_{0},\ldots,\mathbf{r}_{k}\right),\hskip 28.45274pt\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}=\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}_{k}\right).

(4.22)

Lemma 4
.

Let
\{\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\}_{k\geq 0}
and
\{\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}\}_{k\geq 0}
be the i.i.d. standard Gaussian vector sequences defined in Lemma
3
. Then, with respect to the Gaussian equivalent recursion in Algorithm
5
, define

\begin{split}\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}=\sqrt{\rho_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0},&\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r+1}\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{r}^{\mathrm{in}}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\\
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}=\sqrt{\rho_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0},&\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{r+1}\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{r}^{\mathrm{out}}+\sqrt{\rho_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}.\end{split}

(4.23)

Similarly define
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
. With these definitions, we have

1.

\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}=\sum_{r=0}^{k}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr},\qquad\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}=\sum_{r=0}^{k}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{pr}}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{r}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pr}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pr},

(4.24)

where for
k\geq 1
and
0\leq r\leq k-1
,
[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}
and
[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{r}
are defined recursively as

[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}=\sum_{i=r}^{k-1}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pi}]_{r}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i+1},\qquad\text{ and }\qquad[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{r}=\sum_{i=r}^{k-1}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pi}]_{r}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{i+1},

(4.25)

with
[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{k}=[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{k}=1
for all
k\geq 0
.

2.

Let
\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}=\left(\accentset{*}{\mathbf{r}}_{0},\ldots,\accentset{*}{\mathbf{r}}_{k}\right)
where
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{r}}_{j}=(\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j},\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j})
are defined as above for the ideal variables
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}
and
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}
. Then we have that

\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}=\left(\mathbf{d}_{0},\ldots,\mathbf{d}_{k}\right),

(4.26)

where
\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}
is defined in (
4.22
) with

\mathbf{d}_{k}=\left(\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr},\,\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{r}\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pr},\,\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]_{r}\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qr},\,\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}]_{r}\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qr}\right).

(4.27)

Furthermore, for all
k\geq 0
, we have that, conditional on
\left(\mathbf{w}_{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right)
,

\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k}.

(4.28)

3.

For all
i\geq 1
, we have
\left([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i},\ldots,[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i},\ldots,[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\ldots,P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\ldots,P_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)
where
(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}})
and
(P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\ldots,P_{k}^{\mathrm{out}})
are independent, zero-mean, jointly Gaussian vectors (and hence the right hand side is itself jointly Gaussian).

Analogous statements for (1.)-(3.) hold for the
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}
variables.

Recall that we introduced the Gaussian equivalent recursion in Algorithm
5
as an idealized ‘Gaussian’ version of the GVAMP general recursion in Algorithm
3
. Lemma
4
makes this precise. Indeed,
Lemma
4
introduces a recursion in (
9.3
) that is shown to be purely Gaussian in point (3.), in that its iterates are elementwise equal in distribution to Gaussian vectors who covariance structure can be given exactly.
We see from point (2.) of the lemma, that the difference between the terms in Algorithm
5
(given in
\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}
) and the purely Gaussian terms from (
9.3
) (collected in
\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}
) is only in the deviation terms (see (
9.7
)). Moreover, the terms of Algorithm
5
(given in
\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}
) are equal in distribution to those from Algorithm
3
(given in
\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k}
). Taken together, this shows that the difference between the GVAMP general recursion and the purely Gaussian recursion can be summarized via the deviation terms. The major technical part of the proof, given in Lemma
5
is in showing that these deviation terms concentrate exponentially fast to zero, so that the dynamics of the GVAMP general recursion can be characterized by studying the purely Gaussian equivalent.

Before proving Lemma
4
, we note that the Gaussian vectors used to characterize the dynamics of the purely Gaussian recursion in point (3.), namely
(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/out},\ldots,P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}})
and
(Q_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}},\ldots,Q_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}})
, may be explicitly defined in terms of the
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}
variables defined in Lemma
3
as follows. For
k\geq 1
,

\begin{split}P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{pk}]_{r+1}P_{r}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}+\sqrt{\rho_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}]_{1},\quad Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{qk}]_{r+1}Q_{r}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}+\sqrt{\rho_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}]_{1},\end{split}

(4.29)

with initializations
P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}=\sqrt{\rho_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}]_{1}
and
Q_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}=\sqrt{\rho_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}]_{1}
.

Proof.

We prove the results for
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
; the arguments for
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and the output iterates are completely analogous.
To establish the first part, namely the result of (
9.4
), we proceed by induction. First consider the base case
k=0
. We have
[c^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{0}=1
by definition (
9.5
), and thus the statement in (
9.4
) reduces to
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
, which is just the definition of
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
in (
9.3
).

Now assume the statement in (
9.4
) holds for
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}}
for
0\leq j\leq k-1
. Then, combining the definition of
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}
in (
9.3
) with the induction hypothesis, we get

\displaystyle\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r+1}\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{r}^{\mathrm{in}}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r+1}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}]_{j}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right]+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\left[\sum_{r=j}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r+1}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}]_{j}\right]\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j}\,\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}.

as needed, where the final equality uses the definition of
\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
in (
9.5
).

For the second part, we observe that (
9.6
) becomes trivially true in light of the first result where we showed, for example, that
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}=\sum_{r=0}^{k}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}
, and the Gaussian Algorithm
5
step (
2
) definition, for which
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\sum_{r=0}^{k}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}}\,[\mathbf{c}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{r}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}+\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}
.

To establish the distributional equalities in (
9.8
) of part (3.), we make repeated use of two facts:

Fact 1
.

Let
\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}
and
\mathbf{y},\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}}
be random vectors. Then we have:

(A)

If
\mathbf{y}\mid_{\mathbf{x}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
, then
(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{y}})
.

(B)

If
\mathbf{y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
and
\mathbf{x}
is independent of
\mathbf{y}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
, then
f(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}f(\mathbf{x},\widetilde{\mathbf{y}})
for any measurable
f
.

Now we can show
\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k}
given in (
9.8
) inductively. For the initialization step, we show
\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{0}=\mathbf{r}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}_{0}=\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{0}
where by (
4.21
) we have that
\mathbf{r}_{0}=\left(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}\right)
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}_{0}=\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}\right).
We prove the distributional equality for the
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}
terms, while the proof for
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}
follows similarly.
By
Condition 0
and the definition of
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
, we have that

\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\lvert_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\lvert_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,[\mathbf{c}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{0}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}+[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{0}\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}.

To see the equivalence of the two terms above, notice that
[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{0}=1
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}=\mathbf{O}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}=\mathbf{I}
by definition, and
\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}=\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
since the GVAMP recursion and general recursion use identical initialization
\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
. Furthermore, observe that
\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}
are independent since
\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{V}
are independent, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}
are independent since
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
and
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
are independent (by definition), and
\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}
are independent by
Condition 0
.

Next, by definition, conditioning on
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
is equivalent to conditioning on
\sigma\left(\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)
(since
\mathbf{v}_{0}
is a deterministic function of
\mathbf{p}_{0}
conditional on the disturbance vectors; see Algorithm
3
). So Lemma
3
gives that

\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\mid_{\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}}=\sqrt{\rho_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\mathbf{O}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q0}.
Furthermore,
\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}
are again conditionally independent given
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
since
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{U}
are independent, so the above holds for
(\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}})
jointly. Together, this implies

\left(\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\sqrt{\rho_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{O}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0},\sqrt{\rho_{q0}^{\mathrm{out}}}\mathbf{O}_{q0}^{\mathrm{out}}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{out}}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q0}\right),

by (A) in Fact
1
. Now note that
\mathbf{O}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
\bm{\Delta}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
are deterministic functions of
(\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{out}})
, and thus the entire above vector is also a function of these variables. Furthermore, by Lemma 3,
(\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}})
and
(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{out}})
are independent. Likewise,
(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}})
and
(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{out}})
are independent since the former only depends on
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
and
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
. Thus, equality in distribution is maintained if we substitute
(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}})
for
(\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}})
in the right side of the above by (B) in Fact
1
. But this is just
(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}})
by the definitions in the Gaussian Algorithm
5
and the fact that
[\mathbf{c}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}]_{0}=1
. This completes the proof of the base case.

For the inductive step, assume that
\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1}
for some
k\geq 2
.
First we will establish that

\left(\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k-1},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right).

To do this, observe that

\displaystyle\left(\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1},\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{\ell+1}\,\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{\ell+1}\,\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)

(4.30)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Bigg(\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k-1},\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{\ell+1}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{\ell+1}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\Bigg),

(4.31)

where (
4.30
) follows from (A) in Fact
1
(using that conditioning on
\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1}
is equivalent to conditioning on
\mathcal{P}_{k-1}
because the additional
\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{v}
terms that generate the latter are deterministic functions of
\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1}
) and the conclusions of Lemma
3
(particularly, that
\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}
are independent given
{\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1}}
), and where (
4.31
) follows from substituting
(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1})
for
(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\ldots,\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1})
. This substitution is justified by (B) in Fact
1
and the induction hypothesis as
\left(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)
is independent of
(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\ldots,\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1})\subset\mathcal{P}_{k-1}
by construction, and independent of
\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k-1}
as
\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k-1}
only depends on

\left(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0},\ldots\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p(k-1)},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q0},\ldots,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q(k-1)}\right).

Next observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}

\displaystyle[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{\ell+1}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{\ell+1}\,\left[\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p\ell}]_{j}\left(\sqrt{\rho_{pj}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}]\right)\right]+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

(4.32)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\left[\sum_{\ell=j}^{k-1}\left[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right]_{\ell+1}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p\ell}]_{j}\right]\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pj}^{\mathrm{in}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right)+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}+\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j}\sqrt{\rho_{pj}^{\mathrm{in}}}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pj}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j}\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}

(4.33)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},

(4.34)

where (
4.32
) follows from the definitions of
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}}
in Algorithm
5
, (
4.33
) follows from the definitions of the constants
[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j}
, and (
4.34
) again follows from the definition of
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}
in Algorithm
5
. The same reasoning applies to
\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}
.

Thus, as claimed, plugging these into the above gives

\left(\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\widetilde{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}_{k-1},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right).
Next we need to extend this to the
\left(\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)
variables. The proof of this works in exactly the same way as above, conditioning now on
(\underline{\mathbf{r}}_{k-1},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}})
and applying Lemma 2 with (A) and (B) above to get the proper joint distributional equality.

For the third part, we claim

\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}\right).

As a first step, we show that the two above vectors generate the same
\sigma
-algebras. For this it suffices to show that the right is a function of the left. (The reverse is immediate since the
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}
matrices (which are equal to their
\mathbf{O}
equivalents defined in (
4.8
)) are functions of the
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{i},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}
, and these are functions of the right hand side.) We can prove this inductively. For the first component, this is obvious since
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}=\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
is a deterministic orthogonal matrix. Now suppose the statement is true for all components up to
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)r}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p/q)r}^{\mathrm{in}}
. Since
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)r}
is a function of the
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)j}
for
j<r
, it follows by the induction hypothesis that
[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)r}]^{-1}
is a function of the prior
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)j}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p/q)j}^{\mathrm{in}}
components. But then
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p/q)r}^{\mathrm{in}}=[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)r}]^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)r}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p/q)r}^{\mathrm{in}}
can be written as a function of the components of the left hand vector up through
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)r}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p/q)r}^{\mathrm{in}}
, as claimed.

Now we can prove the asserted distributional equality inductively using (A) in Fact
1
by establishing distributional equality for each component conditional on the prior components. For the base case, again we know that
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
is a deterministic orthogonal matrix, so by standard properties of Gaussian random vectors we have that
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}
. Next suppose that the statement is true up through
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)(r-1)}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p/q)(r-1)}^{\mathrm{in}}
. Then conditioning on

\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p(r-1)}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p(r-1)}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q(r-1)}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q(r-1)}^{\mathrm{in}}\right),

is equivalent to conditioning on

\left(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p(r-1)}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q(r-1)}^{\mathrm{in}}\right),

(4.35)

by the induction hypothesis. But, conditional on the above,
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)r}
is a deterministic orthogonal matrix (as it depends only on
\{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{i},\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r-1}
, which are functions of (
4.35
)). Together, this gives that
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}}\mid_{\{\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pi}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pi}^{\mathrm{in}}\}_{i=0}^{r-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}}\mid_{\{\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pi}^{\mathrm{in}}\}_{i=0}^{r-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}.
Putting this together, we find

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{pr}^{\mathrm{in}}\right),

where the first equality follows from the above and (A) in Fact
1
and the second because
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}
is independent of
\left(\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q0}^{\mathrm{in}},\ldots,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{q(r-1)}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p(r-1)}^{\mathrm{in}}\right)
using (B) in Fact
1
. The same distributional equality follows in the same way for the vectors of output variables.

Now we can proceed with the proof of the statement in part (iii). For the base case of
k=0
, we have
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}.
Because the
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}=\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
are deterministic orthogonal matrices, we have that

\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\rho_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}N(0,\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}),

and similarly for the output case. The independence of
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}
follows because
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}
are independent by definition.

To prove the induction step, assume the statement holds for independent Gaussian vectors
(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\ldots,P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k-1})
and
(P^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},\ldots,P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k-1})
(whose values are given in (
4.29
)). We first notice that the induction hypothesis along with the definition of
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
in (
9.3
) and the above implies that

\displaystyle\left([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i},\ldots,[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\left([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i},\ldots,\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{r}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i},\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{r}[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{r}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\,[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\ldots,\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{pk}]_{r}P^{\mathrm{in}}_{r}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i},\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{pk}]_{r}P^{\mathrm{out}}_{r}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\,[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},\ldots,\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{pk}]_{r}P^{\mathrm{in}}_{r}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\,[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i},\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}[\bm{\beta}_{pk}]_{r}P^{\mathrm{out}}_{r}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\,[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}]_{i}\right),

where, on the right-hand side, we treat
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
as independent of the
P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\ldots,P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}
(as the
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}
are independent of
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
for
i<k
, which follows directly from the definition of the ideal variables
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
). In particular, this shows that the last expression is jointly Gaussian (e.g., by Cramer-Wold). The zero-mean property and the independence of the input and output variables follows immediately from the definition of the
\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}
variables. Thus this completes the proof of the third part.

∎

4.4
General State Evolution

Next we define a scalar recursion that is the analog of the GVAMP state evolution in (
3.5
), but for the general algorithm in Algorithm
3
.
One consequence of our general concentration result, Lemma
5
, is that the iterates
\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
\mathbf{q}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
converge empirically to the zero-mean Gaussian variables
P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
and
Q_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
, respectively, with both defined in
(
4.29
)
. The variances of these Gaussian variables are completely characterized by the state evolution equations in (
4.36
). The general algorithm state evolution is given by the following recursive system for
k\geq 0
:

\begin{split}\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}&=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}\right]^{\prime}\left(P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)\right\},\hskip 42.67912pt\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}=\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\left(\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right),\\
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}&=\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)^{-2}\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}\right]^{2}\left(P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)-\left[\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right]^{2}\tau_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right\}\right],\\
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}&=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}\right]^{\prime}\left(Q_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},Q_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{q},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right)\right\},\hskip 42.67912pt\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p(k+1)}=\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\left(\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right),\\
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p(k+1)}&=\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right)^{-2}\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}\right]^{2}\left(Q_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},Q_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{q},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right)-\left[\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right]^{2}\tau_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right\}\right].\end{split}

(4.36)

The initial terms of this recursion
\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
and the random variable
W^{p}
are well-defined by
Condition 0
, and the terms
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}
are initialized with
\rho_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
, which we have thought of as a free parameter of Algorithm
5
and which we will define explicitly along with the other limiting quantities in the next section. In terms of these state evolution quantities, we define two more sequences of random variables. For
k\geq 0
:

\begin{split}U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k+1}=\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right)^{-1}\left[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}\left(Q_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},Q_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{q},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right)-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right],\\
V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}=\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)^{-1}\left[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}\left(P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right],\end{split}

(4.37)

with
Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
and
P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
defined above in (
4.29
), and where
U_{0}
is the random variable determined by
Condition 0
to which the initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{0}
concentrates.

Lemma
5
part
(d)
(which we prove in what follows) implies that
{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\|^{2}}/{N}
concentrates on
\mathbb{E}\{[P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{2}\}
and part
(e)
implies that
{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\|^{2}}/{N}
concentrates to
\mathbb{E}\{[U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{2}\}
.

Following
[
36
]
, we can use these results to see that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\{[P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{2}\}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\|^{2}}{N}&=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\|^{2}}{N}\\
