&=\frac{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}\left(Q_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},Q_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{q},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right)-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\Big)^{2}\Big]}{(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk})^{2}},\end{split}

(4.38)

where the second equality in the above follows because
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
, by definition in Algorithm
3
; hence,
\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\|^{2}=(\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}=\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\|^{2}
.
Now expanding terms and applying Stein’s lemma along with the state evolution definitions in (
4.36
) shows that indeed
\mathbb{E}\{[P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{2}\}=\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
. The same reasoning can be applied to show that
\mathbb{E}\{[Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{2}\}=\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
.

We can also show, assuming again the result of Lemma
5
, that the GVAMP state evolution (
3.5
) is equivalent to the general state evolution (
4.36
) under the translation defined in (
4.1
) - (
4.5
). The equivalences
\overline{\alpha}_{1k}=\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\overline{\beta}_{1k}=\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
follow directly from the definitions of the sensitivity functions
A_{x1}
and
A_{z1}
and the definitions in (
4.2
). The equivalences
\sigma^{2}_{2k}=\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
and
\rho^{2}_{2k}=\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
follow similarly. Then
\overline{\gamma}_{2k}=\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
and
\overline{\tau}_{2k}=\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
follows directly from the definition of the
\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
above.

The equivalences
\overline{\alpha}_{2k}=\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
and
\overline{\beta}_{2k}=\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
follow from as the definitions of the sensitivity functions
A_{x2}
and
A_{z2}
are the limits of the averages of the derivatives of
f_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
f_{q}^{\mathrm{out}}
respectively (again by the definitions in (
4.2
)), and these averages converge to
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
since we have empirical convergence of the arguments. (Indeed, in this case the derivatives do not depend on the
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
, so we only need empirical convergence of the
\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}
, which is guaranteed by
Condition 0
.)

Then, the equivalences
\sigma^{2}_{1k}=\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\rho^{2}_{1k}=\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
are established by the definitions (
4.2
), the uniformly Lipschitz property of
g_{x2}
and
g_{z2}
, and the fact that the i.i.d. sequences
\mathbf{r}_{2}
and
\mathbf{p}_{2}
in the definition of the error functions converge empirically to the proper normal variables. Finally, the equivalences
\overline{\gamma}_{qk}=\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\overline{\tau}_{1k}=\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
follow from the definitions of the
\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
functions above.

4.5
Definitions of Limiting Quantities

The statements of lemmas
3
and
4
are valid for any choice of parameters
\bm{\beta}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
,
\bm{\beta}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
,
\rho_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
, and
\rho_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
, and the exact values of these parameters have yet to be specified. To make our analysis work, we choose these parameters to be the limiting values of the quantities that occur in the discrepancy terms
\bm{\Delta}
defined in Lemma
3
, so that these discrepancy terms concentrate around zero.

To formally define these parameters, we first introduce come intermediate quantities: for
k\geq 0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}=(\mathbb{E}[U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}],\ldots,\mathbb{E}[U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]),\\
&\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}=(\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}],\ldots,\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k-1}V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]),\end{split}

(4.39)

and define matrices
\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk},\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
elementwise as

\begin{split}\left[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right]_{ij}=\mathbb{E}\left[U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}\right],&\qquad\qquad\left[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right]_{ij}=\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}\right],\end{split}

(4.40)

where the
U
and
V
variables are defined in
(
4.37
)
. In terms of these quantities, we define our parameters as follows: for
k\geq 1

\begin{split}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}&=[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{u(k-1)}]^{-1}\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk},\quad\quad\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}=\mathbb{E}\{[U^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{2}\}-(\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk})^{T}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{u(k-1)}]^{-1}\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk},\\
\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}&=[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{v(k-1)}]^{-1}\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk},\quad\quad\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}=\mathbb{E}\{[V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{2}\}-(\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk})^{T}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{v(k-1)}]^{-1}\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}.\end{split}

(4.41)

These parameters are initialized as
\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}=\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q0}=0
,
\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
given by
Condition 0
, and
\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q0}=\mathbb{E}\left\{[V^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]^{2}\right\}
.

These definitions may appear circular at first, as the definitions of the
P
and
Q
variables in Lemma
4
depend on these parameters, which are then defined in terms of moments involving the
P
and
Q
. However, it is easy to check that the definitions can be made recursively, avoiding any circular dependence. For instance
U_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
is determined by the initial condition
\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
. Since
\bm{\beta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}=0
and
\rho_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
is also determined by the initial conditions,
P_{0}
is well-defined in terms of
U_{0}
. Then it is easy to check from the definition and state evolution equations that
V_{0}
is well-defined in terms of
P_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
is well-defined in terms of
V_{0}
. Continuing in this fashion allows us to recursively build up the sequences of
P
,
Q
,
U
, and
V
variables in terms of the previously defined variables.

Finally, we define limiting precision matrices. Let
\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{u0}=[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{u0}]^{-1}
and for
k\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{matrix}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}]^{-1}&0\\
0&[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{v(k-1)}]^{-1}\end{matrix}\right],\qquad\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}=\left[\begin{matrix}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}]^{-1}&0\\
0&[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}]^{-1}\end{matrix}\right],

(4.42)

Lemma 5
.

Throughout the lemma, we use
c
and
C
to denote constants that do not depend on the iteration number, but which may vary in their exact value between usages. For
t\geq 0
, let

\begin{split}C_{k}=C^{2k}(k!)^{16},\quad\qquad&c_{k}=\frac{1}{c^{2k}(k!)^{26}},\quad\qquad C^{\prime}_{k}=C^{k}(k+1)^{8},\quad\qquad c^{\prime}_{k}=\frac{c_{k}}{c(k+1)^{15}}.\end{split}

(4.43)

(a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\|^{2}\geq\epsilon\right)\leq Ck^{2}C^{\prime}_{k-1}e^{-cc^{\prime}_{k-1}n\epsilon/k^{4}},\qquad\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\|^{2}\geq\epsilon\right)\leq Ck^{2}C_{k-1}e^{-cc_{k-1}n\epsilon/k^{4}}.

(b)

For
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{k+2}\to\mathbb{R}
pseudo-Lipschitz, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi

\displaystyle\left([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1}]_{i},[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k+1}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q}]_{i}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi\left(Q^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},...,Q^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1},Q^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},...,Q^{\mathrm{out}}_{k+1},W_{q}^{\mathrm{in}},W_{q}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)\right\}\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big)\leq Ck^{4}C^{\prime}_{k-1}e^{-cc^{\prime}_{k-1}n\epsilon^{2}/k^{7}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi

\displaystyle\left([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k+1}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p}]_{i}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},...,P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},...,P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k+1},W_{p}^{\mathrm{in}},W_{p}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)\right\}\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big)\leq Ck^{4}C^{\prime}_{k-1}e^{-cc^{\prime}_{k-1}n\epsilon^{2}/k^{7}}.

where the
Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}
are jointly Gaussian with
Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}\sim N(0,\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qi})
and
\mathbb{E}\{Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}Q^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}\}=[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{v,k+1}]_{ij}
, the
P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}
are jointly Gaussian with
P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}\sim N(0,\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pi})
and
\mathbb{E}\{P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{i}P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}\}=[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}]_{ij}
.

We will use Lemma
5
to prove Theorem
1
; details in Section
4.6
. Because the proof of Lemma
5
is quite technical, in the main body of the text, we give a high level discussion in Section
4.7
and relegate the details to Appendix
6
. In particular, Section
4.7
discusses the innovation required to prove Lemma
5
, and more generally, describes differences between the finite sample analyses for generalized AMP algorithms and for standard AMP algorithms, as in
[
38
, Lemma 5]
.

4.6
Theorem
1
Proof

Theorem
1
follows from Lemma
5
part
(b)
. In translating GVAMP (Algorithm
2
) to the general iteration (Algorithm
3
), we set
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\mathbf{r}_{1k}
and
\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}=\gamma_{1k}
in (
4.1
). Along with the definition of
\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{1k}
in Algorithm
2
, namely
\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{1k}=g_{x1}(\mathbf{r}_{1k},\gamma_{1k})
, it follows that

\phi([\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{1k}]_{i},[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})=\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})
, where now quantities on the right are all from Algorithm
3
.
Hence, we bound the probability in (
3.2
) by introducing the limiting state evolution quantities
\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}
defined in (
4.36
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi([\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{1k}]_{i},[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})-\mathbb{E}\{\phi(\hat{X}_{1k},X_{0})\}\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})-\mathbb{E}\{\phi(\hat{X}_{1k},X_{0})\}\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})-\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big)

(4.44)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})-\mathbb{E}\{\phi(\hat{X}_{1k},X_{0})\}\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big).

(4.45)

Now we consider the two terms in (
4.44
) and (
4.45
) separately and for both we give an upper bound of
CC_{k}e^{-cc_{k}N\epsilon^{2}}
to establish the desired result. First, we notice that bound on (
4.45
) follows from Lemma
5
part
(b)
. Indeed,
because
g_{x1}(\cdot,\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}})
is Lipschitz, it is easy to verify that the composition
(p,x)\mapsto\phi(g_{x1}(p,\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),x)
is pseudo-Lipschitz, and because
\mathbf{x}_{0}
concentrates to
X_{0}
at the correct rate, we can apply Lemma
5
part
(b)
to conclude that
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})
concentrates to

\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(g_{x1}\Big(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big),X_{0}\Big)\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(g_{x1}\Big(\sqrt{\tau_{pi}^{\mathrm{in}}}Z,\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big),X_{0}\Big)\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(\hat{X}_{1k},X_{0}\Big)\Big].

In the final equality above we have used that
\hat{X}_{1k}=g_{1}(R_{1k},\bar{\gamma}_{1k})
with
R_{1k}=\sigma_{1k}Z
for
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
independent of
X_{0}
, in addition to the facts that
\tau_{pi}^{\mathrm{in}}=\sigma^{2}_{1k}
, which is discussed when introducing the general state evolution in Section
4.4
and that
\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}=\gamma_{1k}
; hence,
\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}=\overline{\gamma}_{1k}
.

Now we will upper bound the probability in (
4.44
). First, notice that by the pseudo-Lipschitz property of
\phi
, we have that
|\phi(a_{1},b)-\phi(a_{2},b)|\leq L(1+\lVert(a_{1},b)\rVert+\lVert(a_{2},b)\rVert)|a_{1}-a_{2}|
. Moreover, by the Triangle Inequality:

\displaystyle\lVert(a_{1},b)\rVert+\lVert(a_{2},b)\rVert=\lVert(a_{1},b)-(a_{2},b)+(a_{2},b)\rVert+\lVert(a_{2},b)\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert(a_{1},b)-(a_{2},b)\rVert+2\lVert(a_{2},b)\rVert

\displaystyle=|a_{1}-a_{2}|+2\lVert(a_{2},b)\rVert.

Putting this together, we have the following bound.

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})-\phi(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{L}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big[1+\Big|g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}})-g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}})\Big|+2\Big\|\Big(g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i}\Big)\Big\|\Big]\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\Big|g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)-g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)\Big|\\
&\leq L\sqrt{3}\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{N}\|g_{x1}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)-g_{x1}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)\|^{2}+\frac{4}{N}\Big\|\Big(g_{x1}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big),\mathbf{x}_{0}\Big)\Big\|^{2}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|g_{x1}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)-g_{x1}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)\|,\end{split}

(4.46)

where the final inequality follows by Cauchy-Schwarz and Lemma
16
.
To see that (
4.46
) concentrates around zero at the correct rate, notice that it suffices to show

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|(g_{x1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),[\mathbf{x}_{0}]_{i})\|^{2}-c_{g}\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big)\leq CC_{k}e^{-cc_{k}N\epsilon^{2}},

(4.47)

for some universal constant
c_{g}>0
, and

\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)-g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)\Big|^{2}\geq\epsilon^{2}\Big)\leq CC_{k}e^{-cc_{k}N\epsilon^{2}}.

(4.48)

Indeed if these concentration inequalities hold, then applications of Lemmas
9
,
12
, and
11
to (
4.46
) give concentration with the right rates in terms of
N
and
\epsilon
.
For (
4.47
), observe that
g_{x1}(\cdot,\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}})
is uniformly Lipschitz by
Assumption 3
. Thus, the composition,
(p,x)\mapsto\|(g_{x1}(p,\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),x)\|^{2}
is pseudo-Lipschitz, and so with
\text{constant}=\mathbb{E}[\|(g_{x1}(P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}),X_{0})\|]^{2}
the result in (
4.47
) follows by part
(b)
of Lemma (
5
). We also use
Assumption 4
that
\gamma_{pk}
and, hence,
\bar{\gamma}_{pk}
is bounded.
To demonstrate the result in (
4.48
), observe that by
Assumption 3
,
g_{x1}
is uniformly Lipschitz, meaning
|g_{x1}(a,b_{1})-g_{x1}(a,b_{2})|\leq L(1+|a|)|b_{1}-b_{2}|
; hence,
|g_{x1}(a,b_{1})-g_{x1}(a,b_{2})|^{2}\leq 2L^{2}(1+|a|^{2})|b_{1}-b_{2}|^{2}
using Lemma
16
.
Therefore, applying Lemma
16
again,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)-g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}-\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big|^{2}\frac{2L^{2}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big(1+|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}-\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big|^{2}\frac{2L^{2}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big(1+2|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}-\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big|^{2}\Big(2L^{2}+\frac{4L^{2}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}|^{2}\Big).

Using the above abound, we can bound the probability in (
4.47
) as follows:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)-g_{x1}\Big([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big)\Big|^{2}\geq\epsilon^{2}\Big)&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}-\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big|^{2}\Big[1+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}|^{2}\Big]\geq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2L^{2}}\Big)\\
&\hskip-85.35826pt\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}-\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2L}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}-\overline{\gamma}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\Big|\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{2}L}\Big).\end{split}

The first term on the right side of the above has the desired bound by Lemma
6
part
(f)
, proved in Appendix
6
.
The second term is bounded as above by using Lemmas
9
,
12
, and
11
to combine the concentration results for the individual factors and terms that are given by Lemma
6
part
(f)
(for
\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
),
and the following bound given in Lemma
6
part
(d)
:

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\lvert\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}-[\Sigma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{k+1,k+1}\Big\lvert\leq 1\Big)\leq CC_{k}e^{-cc_{k}N}.

4.7
Lemma
5
Proof Discussion

Structurally, Lemma
5
is similar to
[
38
, Lemma 5]
, which is used to establish the AMP analog of Theorem
1
for the linear model in (
1.2
). However, there are a number of differences between the algorithms that lead to important differences in the proofs, discussed here.

(1)
In the finite sample analysis of AMP, one often studies vectors of the form
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}
where
\mathbf{A}
has i.i.d. Gaussian entries and
\mathbf{x}
is deterministic. For a vector
\mathbf{Z}
having i.i.d.
N(0,1)
elements,
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\|\mathbf{x}\|\mathbf{Z}
, since
\|\mathbf{x}\|
is deterministic. Importantly, the elements of
\|\mathbf{x}\|\mathbf{Z}
are independent.

With GVAMP, the analogous object of interest are vectors of the form
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{x}
where
\mathbf{V}
is uniformly distributed on the group of orthogonal matrices. This property of
\mathbf{V}
implies that
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{x}
has a rotationally invariant distribution, or mathematically, for a vector
\mathbf{Z}
having i.i.d.
N(0,1)
elements,
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}({\|\mathbf{x}\|}/{\|\mathbf{Z}\|})\mathbf{Z}.

Thus, we pay for relaxing the Gaussianity condition by picking up a
\|\mathbf{Z}\|
factor in the denominator, causing dependencies in the elements of
({\|\mathbf{x}\|}/{\|\mathbf{Z}\|})\mathbf{Z}
that complicate the concentration arguments. By observing that the Gaussiantiy of
\mathbf{Z}
gives concentration of
\|\mathbf{Z}\|/\sqrt{N}
around
1
, it can be shown that
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{x}
will concentrate around the same limit as
(\|\mathbf{x}\|/\sqrt{N})\mathbf{Z}
, which essentially returns us to the AMP case.

(2)
As in the analysis of GAMP, we need to ensure that the
\mathbf{z}_{0}
vector is available at the output denoising stage of the general recursion (as it is an input to the likelihood denoiser). Adding
\mathbf{z}_{0}
to the disturbance vectors
\mathbf{w}
would create dependence between the disturbance vectors and other quantities which would violate our conditions, so instead we must generate it from
\mathbf{x}
in the general recursion itself. To achieve this, we consider a version of the general recursion where vector iterates are replaced by matrix iterates in order to track both the GVAMP iterates and to recreate
\mathbf{z}_{0}
at each loop through the recursion. This case is not handled explicitly in our proof of Lemma
5
, but in Appendix
9
, we demonstrate that when
\mathbf{x}
is a matrix rather than a vector, we recover a characterization of the form
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{G}^{-1/2}_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{G}^{1/2}_{\mathbf{x}}
, where
\mathbf{Z}
is now a matrix of equivalent dimensions with i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries and
\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{M}}
is the Gram matrix corresponding to matrix
\mathbf{M}
. This further generalizes the expression
({\|\mathbf{x}\|}/{\|\mathbf{Z}\|})\mathbf{Z}
above.

(3)
When studying AMP, one often works with projection matrices
\mathsf{P}_{\mathbf{M}^{\perp}}
, which map vectors into the orthogonal complement of the range of a matrix
\mathbf{M}
. On the other hand, when studying GVAMP, we instead work with the transformations of the form
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{M}}
that have columns spanning
\mathrm{range}(\mathbf{M})^{\perp}
. These are related to the corresponding projections by
\mathsf{P}_{\mathbf{M}^{\perp}}=\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{M}}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{M}}]^{T}
. This creates two main differences in the decomposition in Lemma (
3
).

First, in the deviation terms that describe the distance of the algorithm in finite samples from its idealized (and easily analyzable) counterpart, the transformation
\mathbb{I}-\mathsf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{k}}
that shows up in the AMP case is replaced with the matrices
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times 2k}
, where all we know about this matrix is that it has orthonormal columns that span
\mathrm{range}\Big(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}\Big)
. Notice, on the other hand, the projection matrix
\mathbb{I}-\mathsf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{k}}
is fully specified. However, this knowledge is enough to force images of Gaussian vectors under the transformation given by
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}}
to concentrate around zero, because the images are constrained to live in a
k-
dimensional subspace and
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{vk}}
preserves norms.

The second difference is the presence of the orthogonal matrices
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
and
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
in the decomposition. These appear because there is no direct way to relate
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
and
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
to
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
and
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
. However, these matrices end up having no effect on the resulting joint distribution as they are independent of
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
and
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
, respectively.

(4)
Because the parameters
\gamma_{1k},\gamma_{2k}
and
\tau_{1k},\tau_{2k}
can vary with
N
, so too can the behavior of the denoisers
g_{x1},g_{x2}
and
g_{z1},g_{z2}
. This is in contrast to AMP, where the denoisers can vary by iteration but not with
N
. Furthermore, in the AMP case, the denoisers are Lipschitz in both the iterates and the (analog of) the disturbance vectors
\mathbf{w}
, whereas in the GVAMP case, we must consider a denoiser of the form
[\mathbf{w}]_{i}[\mathbf{q}]_{i}
, which is not Lipschitz in both
[\mathbf{w}]_{i}
and
[\mathbf{q}]_{i}
.

These differences are accounted for by using two different extensions of the Lipschitz condition, the
uniformly
Lipschitz condition and the uniformly bounded conditionally Lipschitz condition. We ultimately view the
\gamma
,
\tau
, and disturbance vectors
\mathbf{w}
as parametrizing families of Lipschitz functions, and these two conditions provide sufficient control to ensure that these Lipschitz functions do not vary too rapidly with the parameters and that the corresponding concentration arguments continue to hold.

(5)
The order of denoising and subtracting the Onsager term is interchanged in AMP and GVAMP. This adds an additional layer of complication in obtaining concentration for
\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
: from Algorithm
3
,

\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}=\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\Big[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p},\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\Big],

with
f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p},\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
failing to be Lipschitz (since
\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
also has a dependence on
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
). This prevents us from simply applying Lemma
5

(b)
when proving concentration results for
\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
. However, as with the
1/\|\mathbf{Z}\|
dependence discussed in
(1)
above, we handle this by using concentration of
\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
to its limit
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}
, allowing one to show that
\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}
will concentrate around the same limit as
\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}}\Big[f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p},\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\Big]
. This function is Lipchitz, so (inductively) applying Lemma
5

(b)
gives the desired concentration.

5
Conclusion and Future Work

This work presents rigorous non-asymptotic performance guarantees for vector approximate message passing (VAMP) and its generalized version GVAMP, characterized through their respective state evolution recursions. We show, in both cases, that the probability of deviation from the state evolution predictions decay exponentially in the problem size
N
up through a certain number of iterations related to
N
. This analysis also implies a similar concentration result for generalized approximate message passing (GAMP) and its state evolution.

We expect these concentration results to extend beyond the generalized models (
1.1
) and assumptions considered in this work to other settings where a state evolution has been rigorously proved to characterize performance of an AMP-style algorithm in the large system limit. For example, when the denoisers are non-separable
[
19
]
, to settings where the distributional parameters of the noise and signal must be learned
[
18
]
, or to AMP algorithms that work under more general assumptions on the measurement matrix
[
11
,
16
,
43
,
34
,
15
]
. Another interesting direction for future study is whether the analysis of Li and Wei
[
25
]
can be used for the type of AMP algorithms studied here to improve the dependence between the problem size and number of iterations for which the concentration results hold.

6
Proof of Lemma
5

To prove Lemma
5
, we prove Lemma
6
below. Notice that part (a) and (b) of Lemma
5
are also part (a) and (b) of Lemma
6
, only now Lemma
6
also has parts (c)-(g) as well.

Lemma 6
.

Throughout the lemma, we use
c
and
C
to denote constants that do not depend on the iteration number, but which may vary in their exact value between usages. The values of
C_{k},C^{\prime}_{k},c_{k},
and

for

are given in (
4.43
).
Let
X_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot\cdot}}{{=}}c
mean

\mathbb{P}(|X_{n}-c|\geq\epsilon)\leq Ck^{4}C^{\prime}_{k-1}\exp\left(-cc^{\prime}_{k-1}n\epsilon^{2}/k^{7}\right),

and let
X_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}c
mean

\mathbb{P}\left(|X_{n}-c|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq Ck^{4}C_{k-1}\exp\left(-cc_{k-1}n\epsilon^{2}/k^{7}\right).

(a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\|^{2}\geq\epsilon\right)\leq CkC^{\prime}_{k-1}\exp\left(-cc^{\prime}_{k-1}n\epsilon/k^{2}\right),

(6.39)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\|^{2}\geq\epsilon\right)\leq CkC_{k-1}\exp\left(-cc_{k-1}n\epsilon/k^{2}\right).

(6.40)

(b)

Let
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{k+2}\to\mathbb{R}
pseudo-Lipschitz, or satisfy the following bounded conditionally pseudo-Lipschitz condition:

(a)

\phi
is continuous in all inputs.

(b)

\phi(\cdot,w)
is pseudo-Lipschitz for each

with PL constants continuous in

.

(c)

The domain of

is compact.

Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi

\displaystyle\left([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1}]_{i},[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k+1}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot\cdot}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi\left(Q^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},...,Q^{\mathrm{in}}_{k+1},Q^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},...,Q^{\mathrm{out}}_{k+1},W\right)\right\},

(6.41)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi

\displaystyle\left([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},...,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot\cdot}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},...,P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},...,P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},W\right)\right\},

(6.42)

where the

are jointly Gaussian with

is any vector independent of the

and
\mathbf{q}
iterates that concentrates exponentially fast (see Definition

) around some limiting variable
W
with finite second moment.

(c)

For all

,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k+1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot\cdot}}{{=}}0,\;\;\;\frac{1}{N}[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot\cdot}}{{=}}0,

(6.43)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}0.

(6.44)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot\cdot}}{{=}}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{v,k+1}]_{j+1,k+1},

(6.45)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{uk}]_{j+1,k+1}.

\displaystyle\;\;\;\frac{1}{N}[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot\cdot}}{{=}}0,

(6.47)

(6.48)

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)&\leq C^{\prime}k^{4}C^{\prime}_{k-1}\exp\left(-nc^{\prime}c^{\prime}_{k-1}\epsilon^{2}/k^{7}\right),\\

(6.58)

Remark 7
.

In the course of the Lemma
6
proof, it often happens that the concentrating value of some quantity is a factor in the rate of concentration for another quantity (e.g. when we apply Lemma
A.3
). In theory, we must therefore take care about the varying magnitudes of these concentrating values as the iteration number changes. However, in our case, we can ignore these issues by bounding the relevant concentrating values independently of
n
and the concentration number.

In particular, it suffices to find upper and lower bounds on
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}
and
\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}
and upper bounds on
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}
for all
k\geq 1
. Our stopping criteria allows us to assume that the
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}
and
\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}
are lower bounded independently of
k
in our concentration analysis. For an upper bound, observe that
\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}\leq\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}
. If either of the
\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{(p/q)k}
is a not bounded, then by the translation
\sigma_{1k}=\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\sigma_{2k}=\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
and Theorem
1
with
\phi(x,y)=(x-y)^{2}
, the state evolution prediction of the mean squared error of
\hat{x}_{1k}
or
\hat{x}_{2k}
will diverge as
k\to\infty
. Similarly, we can relate non-bounded behavior for
\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{(p/q)k}
to predicted divergence of iterates in the state evolution.

Finally, recall that by definition,
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{pk}=\mathbb{E}\{(f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p})^{\prime}(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},W^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})\}.
Now, under the strong concavity assumption (
Assumption 2
),
(f^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p})^{\prime}
is bounded independently of
k
, so we trivially have an upper bound on the size of
\overline{\alpha}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
tat is independent of
k
, and similarly for
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{qk}
.

The proof of Lemma
5
follows by induction on iteration
k
. We will refer to results (
6.39
), (
6.41
), (
6.43
), (
6.45
), (
6.47
), (
6.49
), (
6.51
), (
6.53
), (
6.55
), and (
6.57
) as
\mathbf{Q_{k+1}.(a)}
-
\mathbf{Q_{k+1}.(g)}
and results (
6.40
), (
6.42
), (
6.44
), (
6.46
), (
6.48
), (
6.50
), (
6.52
), (
6.54
), (
6.56
), and (
6.58
) as
\mathbf{P_{k}.(a)}
-
\mathbf{P_{k}.(g)}
. Then the proof proceeds as follows:

1.
Prove
\mathbf{P_{0}}
.

2.
Assuming
\mathbf{P_{0}}
, prove
\mathbf{Q_{1}}
.

3.
Assuming
\mathbf{P_{r-1}}
and
\mathbf{Q_{r}}
for
1\leq\mathbf{r}\leq k
, prove
\mathbf{P_{k}}
.

4.
Assuming
\mathbf{P_{r}}
and
\mathbf{Q_{r}}
for
1\leq\mathbf{r}\leq k
, prove
\mathbf{Q_{k+1}}
.

6.1
Showing
\mathbf{P_{0}}
Holds

\mathbf{P_{0}.(a)}
We prove the result for
\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
and the result for
\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
follows in the same way. From Lemma
3
, recall

\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}=\left(\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\right)\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}.

Therefore, we write

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\sqrt{\epsilon}\right)&=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\right|\geq\sqrt{\epsilon}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\right|\geq\sqrt{\epsilon}\right)\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\right|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-1\right|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2}\right),\end{split}

(6.59)

where step
(a)
follows because
[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}}]^{T}\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}}=\mathbf{I}

and step
(b)
from Lemma
9
using that

\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\right|=\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-\sqrt{\frac{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}{N}}\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|\right|\leq\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\right|+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\left|1-\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\right|.

Now, we bound the first term on the right side of (
6.59
) by
C\exp\left(-cN\epsilon\right)
using
Condition 0
and Lemma
12
along with
the fact that
c
depends on
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}>\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}
by the stopping criteria discussed in
Condition 5
. Similarly, using Lemmas
15
and
12
and that
0<\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}<\infty
, we find

\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-1\right|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-1\right|\geq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{4\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}}\right)\leq C\exp\left(-cN\epsilon/\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\right).

\mathbf{P_{0}.(b)}
We prove the statement using a general vector
\mathbf{w}
, independent of
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{q}
, that concentrates exponentially fast (see Definition
3
) around some limiting variable
W
with finite second moment. When this result is applied in later steps in the induction, this value will be
\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}
.

Now for the proof, first we observe, using Lemma
3
, that

\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\lvert_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\,\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}+\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0},

where we recall that
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
is a length-
N
vector with independent, standard Gaussian entries that are independent of the conditioning sigma-algebra and
\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
is a deterministic orthogonal matrix (since we have
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}=\emptyset
as defined in (
4.8
)). Using the notation defined in Lemma
3
,

\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}=\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}+\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}=\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}.

(6.60)

An analogous representation holds for
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}
.
Therefore, using Lemma
9
, we find

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}&\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},W\right)\right\}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|\phi\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i}+[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i},\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i}+[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\\
&\hskip 227.62204pt-\phi\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i},\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big)\\
&+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i},\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},W\right)\right\}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right),\end{split}

(6.61)

where to make the above well-defined, we continue our convention of letting
[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i}=[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{v0}]_{i}=0

for
M<i\leq N
. Label the terms on the right side of (
6.61
) as
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
. If
\phi
is pseudo-Lipschitz over all inputs jointly, we can bound
T_{1}
using the triangle inequality
and Cauchy-Schwarz as follows:

\displaystyle T_{1}\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{L}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big[1+2\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\left|[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i}\right|+2\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}\left|[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i}\right|+2\left|[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right|+\left|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i}\right|+\left|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i}\right|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 327.20668pt\times\Big[\left|[\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i}\right|+\left|[\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i}\right|\Big]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|+\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\left[1+2\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+2\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+2\frac{\|\mathbf{w}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\right]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4L}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left[\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}\|}{\sqrt{M}}\right]\left[1+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}\|}{\sqrt{M}}+\frac{\|\mathbf{w}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{out}}\|}{\sqrt{M}}\right]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{8L}\right),

where the last inequality follows because
M\leq N
. Now we observe that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|\bm{\Delta}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{M}}\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}\|
concentrate around
0
by
\mathbf{P_{0}.(a)}
,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\|
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{M}}\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}\|
concentrate around
1
by Lemmas
12
and
15
, and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|\mathbf{w}\|
concentrates around
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}W^{2}}
by the assumption of the part (b) statement. The overall concentration of term
T_{1}
then follows from application of Lemmas
11
and
9
. We note that the rate of concentration will depend on the values
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0},\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0},
and
\mathbb{E}W^{2}
all of which are assumed to be finite, with
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
and
\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
not too small by the stopping criteria discussed in
Condition 5
; so all are absorbed into the constants.

If on the other hand
\phi
satisfies the bounded conditionally pseudo-Lipschitz property, then the functions
f_{i}=\phi\left(\cdot,\cdot,[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)
are each pseudo-Lipschitz with PL constants that satisfy a common upper bound (since the PL constants are continuous in the
[\mathbf{w}]_{i}
, which are themselves contained in a compact set by defintion). In this case, we can apply the exact same strategy as above with the
f_{i}
in place of the
\phi
and obtain an analogous bound where the
\|\mathbf{w}\|/\sqrt{N}
term is dropped and
L
is now an upper bound for the PL constants of the
f_{i}
.

For
T_{2}
, note that for all
i
for which the expression is defined,
we have by part 1 of Lemma
4
that

[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i}=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\bar{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i},

(6.62)

where the final equality uses that
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}=\mathbf{O}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
, due to the initialization of Algorithm
5
(see the discussion following the presentation of the algorithm) and (
6.60
); hence,
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}\bar{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}\overset{d}{=}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
.
Moreover, by part 3 of Lemma
4
,

[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}.

(6.63)

Thus, if
\phi
is jointly pseudo-Lipschitz in all inputs, we can apply Lemma
17
to get concentration for
T2
.

If instead
\phi
satsifies the conditionally bounded pseudo-Lipschitz condition, then letting
\mathbb{E}_{P}
denote the expectation with respect to
(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}})
and using Lemma
9
, we get the further inequality

\begin{split}T_{2}&=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i},\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},W\right)\right\}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}]_{i},\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\phi\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\right\}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\phi\left(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},W)\right\}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right),\end{split}

(6.64)

Let the first term on the right side of (
6.64
) be denoted
T21
and the second term be denoted
T22
. For
T21
, note that if
f_{i}(p_{1},p_{2})=\phi(p_{1},p_{2},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})
, then by the conditionally bounded pseudo-Lipschitz condition, the
f_{i}
are pseudo-Lipschitz with constant continuous in
[\mathbf{w}]_{i}
. But since the
[\mathbf{w}]_{i}
are bounded, so too are these constants. Thus, the
f_{i}
are all pseudo-Lipschitz with some common constant
B
, and Lemma
17
again gives concentration for T21.

For T22, the continuity of
x\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\phi(P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},x)\right\}
and the fact that the
[\mathbf{w}]_{i}
are bounded i.i.d. implies that the terms of
T22
are again bounded i.i.d.. Thus, the desired concentration rate is given by Hoeffding’s inequality.

\mathbf{P_{0}.(c)}
The result follows using
\mathbf{P_{0}.(b)}
with the pseudo-Lipschitz functions
\phi(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w)=p^{\mathrm{in}}w
and
\phi(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w)=p^{\mathrm{out}}w
along with the facts that
\mathbb{E}\{P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}\}=0
and
\mathbb{E}\{P_{0}^{\mathrm{out}}W_{p}^{\mathrm{out}}\}=0
, since
W^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p}
and
P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
are independent.

\mathbf{P_{0}.(d)}

The result follows using
\mathbf{P_{0}.(b)}
with the pseudo-Lipschitz functions
\phi(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w)=(p^{\mathrm{in}})^{2}
and
\phi(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w)=(p^{\mathrm{out}})^{2}
.

\mathbf{P_{0}.(e)-(f)}
These are proved in exactly the same way as
\mathbf{P_{k}.(e)}
and
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
in Section
6.3
with
j=k=0
throughout and where references to
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
are replaced by our assumption that the initial sequences
\gamma^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
converges to
\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
. Thus, we omit the details here and refer to the proof of the more general case of
\mathbf{P_{k}.(e)}
and
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
.

\mathbf{P_{0}.(g).(i)}
Because
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}=\emptyset
is empty,
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}
is a determinsitic orthogonal matrix; therefore,
\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}
where the second equality follows as
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
in Algorithm
3
. The result then follows from the assumed concentration of
\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
and the definition of
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}
in (
4.41
).

\mathbf{P_{0}.(g).(ii)}
By (
4.2
),
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}=\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}
. Thus, the invertibility of
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
just requires that
[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}\neq 0
, which is true with probability one. Moreover, by
\mathbf{P_{0}.(d)}
, we know that
[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}/N
concentrates to
\mathbb{E}\left[P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]^{2}
and since, by
\mathbf{P_{0}.(d)}
and (
4.42
),

\left[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{u0}\right]^{-1}=\mathbb{E}\left[U_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]^{2}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\|^{2}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[P_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]^{2},

the desired result follows by Lemma
13
. Application of Lemma
13
will put a power of
\tau^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{p0}
in the numerator of the rate of concentration; this can be lower bounded by the stopping time assumption.

\mathbf{P_{0}.(g).(iii)}
We prove the ‘
\mathrm{in}
’ version of the result, while the ‘
\mathrm{out}
’ version follows correspondingly. Recall,
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0}=\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
by (
4.2
) and
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N-1}
is an orthogonal matrix with columns that form an orthonormal basis for
\mathrm{range}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p0})^{\perp}=[\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{\perp}
. Thus, the matrix
[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\;\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}/\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|]\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
is orthogonal and

\|\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}=\left\|\left[\begin{matrix}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\\
\|\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|^{-1}[\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\end{matrix}\right]\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\right\|^{2}=\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|^{2}+\frac{([\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}})^{2}}{\|\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}\|^{2}}.

(6.65)

Rearranging, this gives us

\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}-\frac{([\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}}{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}}.

(6.66)

Finally, recalling that
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}=\mathbb{E}\{[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{2}\}
from (
4.41
) and using Lemma
9
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}-\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\{[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{2}\}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{([\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}}{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

(6.67)

The first term concentrates by
\mathbf{P_{0}.(e)}
since
\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}=\mathbb{E}\{[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{2}\}
by definition.
The second term concentrates by
\mathbf{P_{0}.(d)}
,
\mathbf{P_{0}.(f)}
, Lemma
13
, and Lemma
11
.

\mathbf{P_{0}.(g).(iv)}
To show that
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}
is invertible with high probability, recall that
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}=[\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\;\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]=[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\;\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]
by (
4.2
); therefore,
\det\left([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}\right)=\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}-([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}).

Noting that
\|\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\|=\|\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\|
, we have by
\mathbf{P_{0}.(e)(f)}
,
Condition 0
, and Lemmas
9
and
10
that this concentrates around
(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0})(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0})>0
.
Then we have

([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1})^{-1}=\frac{1}{\det\left([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v1}\right)}\left[\begin{matrix}[\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}&-[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\\
-[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}&[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\end{matrix}\right].

(6.68)

By similar reasoning, namely via
\mathbf{P_{0}.(d)(e)(f)}
and Lemma
10
, the entries of (
6.68
) have the desired concentrate concentrating constant given below using the definition of
\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}
given in (
4.42
).

\left[\begin{matrix}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0})^{-1}&0\\
0&(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0})^{-1}\end{matrix}\right]=\bm{\Pi}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}.

6.2
Showing
\mathbf{Q_{1}}
Holds

\mathbf{Q_{1}.(a)}
First, recall that by Lemma
3
,

\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}=\frac{[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}}{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}+\left(\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\right)\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}.

Labeling the above three terms
T_{1}
-
T_{3}
, we have
\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|^{2}=\|T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}\|^{2}\leq 3\|T_{1}\|^{2}+3\|T_{2}\|^{2}+3\|T_{3}\|^{2}
by Lemma
16
. Hence, it suffices to derive bounds for
\mathbb{P}(\frac{1}{N}\|T_{i}\|^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{9})
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
by Lemma
9
.

For
T_{1}
, note that
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
by Algorithm
3
step (
4
), and so
\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}=\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}
as
\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{I}
. Thus
\|T_{1}\|^{2}=\frac{([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}}{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{4}}\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}=\frac{([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}}{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}}
and an upper bound is given in (
6.67
).

For
T_{2}
, observe that
\|\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|^{2}=[\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}]^{T}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}=\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|^{2}
using
[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}=\mathbf{I}
and therefore

\displaystyle\|T_{2}\|^{2}=\left\lvert\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\right\lvert^{2}\|\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\right\lvert^{2}\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|\right\lvert^{2}.

By Lemma
9
along with
\mathbf{P_{0}.(g).(iii)}
, Lemma
15
, and Lemma
12
, we have the desired bound using

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|T_{2}\|\geq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{9}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left|\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{p0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\right|+\sqrt{\frac{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}{N}}\left|\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|-1\right|\geq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{9}}\right).

Finally, notice that
\|T_{3}\|=\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}}\|\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|
. Observe that because
\mathrm{rank}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0}\right)=1
, the matrix
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}
is just a unit column vector. Furthermore,
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}
has length
1
(by definition); thus, is a standard normal. Combined, these give us that

\|\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\|=|\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}|\|\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}\|=|\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}|\breve{Z}|

with
\breve{Z}\sim N(0,1)
. So the above concentrates by Lemma
14
.

\mathbf{Q_{1}.(b)}
The first part of the proof of part
(b)
follows as in
\mathbf{P_{0}}
.
Using Lemma (
4
) part 2, we have
[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i}\overset{d}{=}[\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i}
; hence, by Lemma
9
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\phi\left(Q_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},Q_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}\right)\right|\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\phi\left(Q_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},Q_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}\right)\right|\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\phi\left(Q_{0}^{\mathrm{in}},Q_{0}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}\right)\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

Now, using Lemma
17
/Hoeffding’s inequality and Lemma
4
part 3, we can bound the second term above using exactly the same strategy as in the proof of
\mathbf{P_{0}.(b)}
. For the first term, if
\phi
is pseudo-Lipschitz in all inputs, we can apply the pseudo-Lipschitz property and Lemma
4
part 2 to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\phi\left([\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)-\phi\left([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}\right)\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{L}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}]_{i}|+|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q0}]_{i}|]\left[1+2|[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i}|+2|[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i}|+2|[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}|+|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}]_{i}|+|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q0}]_{i}|\right]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(L\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\frac{|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}]_{i}|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q0}]_{i}|}{\sqrt{M}}\right]\left[1+2\frac{|[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i}|}{\sqrt{N}}+2\frac{|[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i}|}{\sqrt{M}}+2\frac{|[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}]_{i}|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}]_{i}|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{|[\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{out}}_{q0}]_{i}|}{\sqrt{M}}\right]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right),

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
M\leq N
. Now this term can be shown to concentrate in exactly the same way as in
\mathbf{P_{0}.(b)}
, namely applying Cauchy-Schwarz to re-express the above in terms of norms, then applying
\mathbf{Q_{0}.(a)}
to the
\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q0}\|
terms, our concentration assumption to
\|\mathbf{w}^{q}\|
, and Lemmas
4
part 3,
12
, and
15
to the
\|\accentset{*}{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}\|
as in (
6.62
)-(
6.63
).

If instead
\phi
satisfies the conditionally bounded pseudo-Lipschitz condition, then we can apply the analogous strategy to the functions
f_{i}=\phi\left(\cdot,\cdot,\left[\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}\right]_{i}\right)
, which are themselves pseudo-Lipschitz with respect to a common constant (since these constants are a continuous function of the bounded
[\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i}
). The resulting bound drops the
2|[\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i}|/\sqrt{N}
term, but is otherwise unchanged.

Combining these inequalities, this completes the proof of
\mathbf{Q_{0}.(b)}
.

\mathbf{Q_{1}.(c)-(f)}
For the inner product
[\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
, note that
[\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i}=[\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{2i}
for
1\leq i\leq N
, and
(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q})\mapsto[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
is pseudo-Lipschitz of order 2, so by
\mathbf{Q}_{1}.(b)
, we have that this concentrates to
\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{0}U_{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}[Q_{0}]\mathbb{E}[U_{0}]=0
since
Q_{0}
and
U_{0}
are independent by hypothesis and
\mathbb{E}Q_{0}=0
.

For the inner product
[\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{in}}
, we expand the definition of
\mathbf{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{in}}
, obtaining

\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}}\left[[\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}f_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}},\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right)-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}[\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}\right].

Showing that this quantity concentrates around
\mathbb{E}U_{0}U_{1}
uses the definition of
U_{0}
and
U_{1}
along with the previously esablished concentration of
\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
in
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(f)}
, the previously established concentration of
[\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}
(above), and the concentration of

[\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}f_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}},\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{qk}\right),

which follows from
\mathbf{Q_{q}.(b)}
and the fact that the above is pseudo-Lipschitz in
(\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0},\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})
. The details of the argument are entirely parallel to the details of proving
\mathbf{P_{k}.(e)}
, which we present explicitly as that is the more complicated case.

The rest of these follow by applications of
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(b)}
and concentration of
\alpha^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{q0}
in the same way that
\mathbf{P_{0}.(c)}-\mathbf{(f)}
were proved.

\mathbf{Q_{1}.(g).(i)}
We will show that
\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}
concentrates around
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}
. First, observe that

\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{u0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}=[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}-\frac{([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}}{[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}}.

(6.69)

Because
[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}=[\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
, using
\mathbf{P_{0}.(e)}
,
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(e)}
, and
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(f)}
, along with Lemmas
9
and
10
, the above concentrates around
\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{2}=\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}
as desired.

\mathbf{Q_{1}.(g).(ii)}
First observe that
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}
is invertible if and only if

\det([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})=([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})-([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})\neq 0.

Using the fact that
\|\mathbf{q}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\|=\|\mathbf{v}_{0}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\|
, we have by
Conditon 0
,
\mathbf{P_{0}.(e)}
,
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(c)}
and Lemmas
9
and
10
that the above concentrates around
\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0}\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}>0
. Now, by Lemma
10
, we have that

([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}=\frac{1}{\det([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})}\left[\begin{matrix}[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}&-[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\\
-[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}&[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\end{matrix}\right],

will concentrate around the desired constants

\left[\begin{matrix}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0})^{-1}&0\\
0&(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0})^{-1}\end{matrix}\right].

\mathbf{Q_{1}.(g).(iii)}
Recall that
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\;\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]
by (
4.2
) and that
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N-2}
is an orthogonal matrix with columns that form an orthonormal basis for
\mathrm{range}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{\perp}=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\;\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{\perp}
. Thus, the matrix
[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}\;\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
is orthogonal and

\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}=\left\|\left[\begin{matrix}[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\\
[\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\end{matrix}\right]\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\right\|^{2}=\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}+\|[\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}.

(6.70)

Rearranging, and noticing that
\|[\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{v0}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}
, this gives us

\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}-[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}.

(6.71)

Now
\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}
concentrates around
(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})_{22}
by
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(e)}
, and we have

\displaystyle[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}=

\displaystyle([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}\frac{[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}}{\det([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})}+([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}\frac{[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}}{\det([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})}-2([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})\frac{[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}}{\det([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})}.

Label these terms
T_{1}-T_{3}
. We first notice that

\det\left([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}\right)=\|\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\|^{2}-([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}.

By
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(d)(e)(f)}
, with
Condition 0
and Lemmas
9
and
10
, this concentrates to
(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})_{22}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})_{11}>0
.

Then
T_{1}
concentrates around
\frac{(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})_{12}^{2}}{(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})_{11}}
by the above,
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(e)}
,
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(d)}
, and Lemma
10
. Next notice that by the above,
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(f)}
, and Lemma
10
, we have that
T_{2}
concentrates around zero. And similarly by the above,
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(e)}
,
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(f)}
, and Lemma
10
,
T_{3}
concentrates around zero. So we ultimately get that
\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}
concentrates around

(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})_{11}-\frac{(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})_{01}^{2}}{(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})_{00}}=\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1},

where the equality follows using (
4.39
), (
4.40
), and (
4.41
) and which say

\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}=\mathbb{E}\{[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{2}\}-(\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})^{T}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0}]^{-1}\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}=\mathbb{E}\{[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{2}\}-\frac{(\mathbb{E}[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}])^{2}}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0}}=[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]_{11}-\frac{[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]_{10}^{2}}{[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]_{00}}.

\mathbf{Q_{1}.(g).(iv)}
Notice that by (
4.42
) for
k\geq 1
, we have
[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]^{-1}=\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}&0\\
0&\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k-1)}\end{matrix}\right]
, so in particular,
[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]^{-1}=\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}&0\\
0&\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}\end{matrix}\right]
, where these matrices are defined in (
4.40
).
Next, define
\mathbf{F}:=[\mathbf{e}_{2}\;\mathbf{e}_{1}\;\mathbf{e}_{3}]\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
, where
\mathbf{e}_{i}
is the
i^{th}
canonical vector, namely the vector of zeros with a one in the
i^{th}
position, so that for a matrix
\mathbf{A}
, the transformation
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{F}
swaps the second and first column of
\mathbf{A}
. Then, using that
\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}=\mathbb{E}[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]
by (
4.39
),

\begin{split}\mathbf{F}^{T}[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]^{-1}\mathbf{F}&=\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})^{2}]&[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}\;0]\\
\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}\\
0\end{matrix}\right]&[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}]^{-1}\end{matrix}\right].\end{split}

Then, using
\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{F}^{T}=\mathbf{F}^{-1},
so that
(\mathbf{F}^{T}[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0}]^{-1}\mathbf{F})^{-1}=\mathbf{F}\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u0}\mathbf{F}^{T},
the block matrix inversion formula gives

\mathbf{F}\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}\mathbf{F}^{T}=\left[\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}\end{matrix}\right]+\frac{1}{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}}\left[\begin{matrix}1&-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}\;0]\\
-\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}\\
0\end{matrix}\right]&\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}\\
0\end{matrix}\right][\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}\;0]\end{matrix}\right].

(6.72)

In the above, we have used that
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}=\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})^{2}]-(\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})^{2}/\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})^{2}]-[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}\;0]\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q0}[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}\;0]^{T}
and
\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}=\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}/\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}]
by (
4.41
).

Similarly, using that
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\,\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\,\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]
and
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\,\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]
, we get

\mathbf{F}^{T}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})F=\left[\begin{matrix}[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}&[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}\\
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}&[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}\end{matrix}\right].

Letting
\bm{\nu}=([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}
, again by block matrix inversion,
formula gives us

\mathbf{F}^{T}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})^{-1}\mathbf{F}=\left[\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}\end{matrix}\right]+\frac{1}{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}}\left[\begin{matrix}1&-\bm{\nu}^{T}\\
-\bm{\nu}&-\bm{\nu}\bm{\nu}^{T}\end{matrix}\right],

(6.73)

where we use that
\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}-\|\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}=\|\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{2}-[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}([\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}
, using an argument similar to that in (
6.70
)-(
6.71
).

Now our goal is to show that, elementwise, (
6.73
) concentrates on (
6.72
).
By Lemmas
9
and
10
, it suffices to show three results. Namely, that
([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}
concentrates on
\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v0}
, that
\|\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{-2}
concentrates on
[\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}]^{-1}
, and that
\bm{\nu}
concentrates on
[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p1}\;0]^{T}
. Elementwise concentration for
([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}
follows from
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(g).(i)}
, and concentration for
\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{q1}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\|^{-2}
follows from
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(g).(ii)}
along with Lemma
13
. Note that the
\rho
terms are bounded above by our requirement that the state evolution not diverge in
Condition 5
.

Finally, let
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}=([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1})^{-1}
and observe that

\bm{\nu}=\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}\left[\begin{matrix}[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\\
[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}\end{matrix}\right]=\left[\begin{matrix}[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{11}([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})+[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{12}([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})\\
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{21}([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})+[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{22}([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})\end{matrix}\right].

(6.74)

Now we show concentration for the first element of (
6.74
). By
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(e)}
,
[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}
concentrates around
\mathbb{E}[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]
and by
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(g).(ii)}
, we have that
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{11}
concentrates around
1/{\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}]}
, so by Lemma
10
, we have that
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{11}([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})
concentrates around
\frac{\mathbb{E}[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}U^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]}{\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{in}}_{0})^{2}]}=\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}
by (
4.41
). Similarly, we have that
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{12}
concentrates around
0
and
[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}
concentrates around
0
by
\mathbf{Q_{1}.(f)}
. Therefore, using so again by Lemmas
9
, we get that
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{11}([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})+[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{12}([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})
concentrates around
\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}
.

Noting that
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{21}
and
[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}
concentrate around
0
and proceeding similarly gives that
