[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{21}([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})+[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q1}]_{22}([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1})
concentrates around
0
. This completes the proof that the entries of
([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1})^{-1}
concentrate around the entries of
\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u1}
.

6.3
Showing
\mathbf{P_{k}}
Holds

First we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 7
.

For all
1\leq i\leq 2k
and for
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left[([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}-\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\\
0\end{matrix}\right]_{i}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq CkC_{k-1}\exp\left(-ncc_{k-1}\epsilon^{2}/k^{2}\right).

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk})^{-1}
and recall by (
4.2
) that
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}=[\mathbf{U}_{k-1}^{\mathrm{in}}\,\mathbf{Q}_{k-1}^{\mathrm{in}}]
. Then, for
1\leq i\leq 2k
,

\left|\left[([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}-\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\\
0\end{matrix}\right]_{i}\right|\leq\left|\sum_{j=1}^{k}[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{ij}([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j-1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k})-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right|+\left|\sum_{j=1}^{k}[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i,j+k}([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j-1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k})\right|.

(6.75)

Now by
\mathbf{P_{k-1}.(g).(ii)}
, we know that the entries of
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
concentrate on finite values. In particular, we know that if
1\leq i,j,\leq k
, then
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{ij}
concentrates on
[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{q,k-1}]_{ij}=\left[\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)}\right)^{-1}\right]_{ij}
where the equality follows by (
4.42
). Moreover, by
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(e)}
and
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
, we have that
[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j-1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and
[\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j-1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
concentrate on
[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u,k}]_{j,k+1}=\mathbb{E}[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{j-1}U^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]=[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]_{j}
and
0
, respectively.

Therefore, using Lemmas
9
and
10
,
\sum_{j=1}^{k}[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{ij}([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j-1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k})
concentrates on

\sum_{j=1}^{k}[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)})^{-1}]_{ij}(\mathbf{b}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}})_{j}=\left[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)})^{-1}(\mathbf{b}_{uk}^{\mathrm{in}})\right]_{i}=[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}

and
\sum_{j=1}^{k}[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i,j+k}([\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j-1}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k})
concentrates on
0
.

Now if
k+1\leq i\leq 2k
, then we can apply the same analysis, but now for
1\leq j\leq k
, we get
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i,j}
concentrating on
0
, so
\left[([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}
concentrates on
0
, as needed.
∎

\mathbf{P_{k}.(a)}

Letting
\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
, recall by Lemma
3
, that we have

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\left(\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\\
0\end{matrix}\right]\right)+\left(\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right)\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left([\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right)[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{2k}[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right)\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\breve{\mathbf{Z}}_{pk},

where we have used the notation
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}
to indicate the
i^{th}
column of the matrix
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}
.

Therefore, by the triangle inequality, we find

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}}\|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\lvert[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right\lvert\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}\|+\sum_{i=k+1}^{2k}\left\lvert[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right\lvert\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left\lvert\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right\lvert\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|+\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|,

where we have used that
[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}=[\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}=\mathbf{I}
.
Then, using Lemma
9
, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\bm{\Delta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\sqrt{\epsilon}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right|\frac{\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)+\sum_{i=k+1}^{2k}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right|\frac{\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)

(6.76)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right|\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}}\right).

In what follows, we show upper bound for each of the terms on the right side of (
6.76
).

First term of (
6.76
).
Observe that by the definition of
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}
in (
4.2
), for
1\leq i\leq k
, that
\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}\|=\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i-1}\|
. Then for
0\leq j\leq k-1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j+1}-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j+1}\right|\frac{\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,j+1)}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)\\
&\qquad=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j+1}-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j+1}\right|\left[\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk})_{jj}\right|+(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk})_{jj}\right]\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)\\
&\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk})_{jj}\right|\geq\sqrt{\epsilon}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j+1}-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{j+1}\right|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{8k\max\{1,(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk})_{jj}\}}\right).\end{split}

(6.77)

The first term concentrates by
\mathbf{P_{j}.(d)}
(
0\leq j\leq k-1
) and the fact that

\mathbb{E}\left[P_{i}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}P_{j}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]=\lim_{N\to\infty}\left[\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]^{T}\mathbf{p}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\left[\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]^{T}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}=\mathbb{E}\left[U_{i}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}U_{j}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right],

where the first and last equalities follow from
\mathbf{P_{k-1}.(d)}
and
\mathbf{Q_{k-1}.(e)}
, and the middle equality follows from the fact that
\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}
.

Then the second term concentrates by Lemma
7
. We note that the above also implies that
(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk})_{jj}=\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}
, which is upper bounded independently of
N
and
j
by
Condition 5
.

Second term of (
6.76
).
For
k+1\leq i\leq 2k
, we have
\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}\|=\|\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i-(k+1)}\|
. Letting
i^{\prime}=i-(k+1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right|\frac{\|[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]_{(\cdot,i)}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right|\left[\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i^{\prime}}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})_{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\right|+(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})_{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\right]\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)

(6.78)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i^{\prime}}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})_{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\right|\geq\sqrt{\epsilon}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|[\bm{\mu}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}\right|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{8k\max\{1,(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk})_{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\}}\right).

Again, the first term concentrates by
\mathbf{P_{i^{\prime}}.(e)}
where
0\leq i^{\prime}\leq k-1
, and the second term by Lemma
7
.

Third term of (
6.76
).
By Lemma
11
, we find

\begin{split}&P\left(\left|\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right|\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)\\
&\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{qk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{8k}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}{\sqrt{N}}-1\right|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{8k\max\{1,\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\}}\right).\end{split}

(6.79)

The second term concentrates by Lemmas
15
and
12
, and the first term by
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(g).(iii)}
.

Fourth term of (
6.76
).
Finally, recall from Lemma
3
that
\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is a length-
2k
vector. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4k}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{2k}[\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{2}_{i}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{16k^{2}}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2k}\mathbb{P}\left([\breve{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{i}^{2}\geq\frac{N\epsilon}{32k^{3}}\right).

(6.80)

Now the last expression concentrates by Lemma
14
.

\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}

In what follows, the notation
\underline{x}_{k}
means
\left(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\right)
(e.g.,
\underline{P}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}=(P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\ldots,P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k})
and
\left[\underline{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]_{i}=([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i})
).
Using Lemma
4
result (
9.8
) and the triangle inequality,

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\left[\underline{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W)\right\}\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W)\right\}\right|\\
&\leq\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W)\right\}\right|\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\phi\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\phi\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\right|.\end{split}

(6.81)

We label the two terms on the right side of (
6.81
) as T1 and T2. First, we establish concentration for T1. Wwe modify our usual convention and take
[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}
for
i>M
to be i.i.d. copies of
\underline{P}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
. We can do this since
\phi(\cdot,\cdot,[\mathbf{w}]_{i})
does not depend on its second argument for
i>M
. This modification implies that

\left\{\left(\left[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}\right]_{i},\ldots,\left[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}\right]_{i},\ldots,\left[\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}\right)\right\}_{i\geq 1}

are independent samples from a common multivariate normal distribution by part 3 of Lemma
4
. If
\phi
is jointly pseudo-Lipschitz in all inputs, then concentration for T1 already follows directly by Lemma
17
. If instead
\phi
satisfies the conditionally bounded pseudo-Lipschitz property, we further decompose T1 as

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\right\}-\mathbb{E}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},\underline{P}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W)\right\}\right|,

where
\mathbb{E}_{P}
is the expectation with respect to the random variables
(\underline{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\underline{P}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k})
. We label these terms T1a and T1b.

Now, taking
f_{i}=\phi(\cdot,\cdot,[\mathbf{w}]_{i})
, Lemma
17
will give the desired concentration for the first term so long as the
f_{i}
are pseudo-Lipschitz with a common constant. But by the bounded conditionally pseudo-Lischitz condition, the PL constant of
f_{i}
is continuous in
[\mathbf{w}]_{i}
. Since these are themselves bounded, the PL constants must also be bounded by some
B
, which we can take as our common constant.

To get concentration for T1b, we note that
x\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\phi(\underline{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},\underline{P}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},x)\right\}
is continuous in
x
(since
\phi
is continuous in all inputs), and is thus bounded over the domain of
\mathbf{w}
since this domain is compact by definition. Thus, the terms in T1b are bounded and i.i.d., so concentration follows with desired rate by a direct application of Hoeffding’s inequality.

Now we bound T2 of (
6.81
). First, the pseudo-Lipschitz property of
\phi
gives the bound

\displaystyle\left|\phi\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\phi\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq L\left[1+\lVert\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\rVert+\lVert\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\rVert\right]\lVert\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}\right)\rVert

\displaystyle\leq L\left[1+2\lVert\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\rVert+\lVert\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}\rVert+\lVert\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}\rVert\right]\left[\lVert\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}\rVert+\lVert\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}\rVert\right],

where the final inequality uses that
\lVert(\underline{a},\underline{b})\rVert\leq\lVert\underline{a}\rVert+\lVert\underline{b}\rVert
and that

\lVert(\underline{a},\underline{b},w)\rVert=\lVert(\underline{a},\underline{b},w)-(\tilde{\underline{a}},\tilde{\underline{b}},w)+(\tilde{\underline{a}},\tilde{\underline{b}},w)\rVert\leq\lVert(\underline{a}-\tilde{\underline{a}},\underline{b}-\tilde{\underline{b}})\rVert+\lVert(\tilde{\underline{a}},\tilde{\underline{b}},w)\rVert.

Next, applying the above bound, Cauchy-Schwarz, and Lemma
16
, we get

\displaystyle[T2]^{2}=\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\phi\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\phi\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\right|\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big[\frac{L}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(1+2\|(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\|+\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\|+\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}\|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\left(\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\|+\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}\|\right)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8L^{2}}{N}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\|^{2}+\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}\|^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\left[1+\frac{4}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\|^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2}\right].

Next, using the state evolution in (
4.36
) and the discussion around (
4.38
), if
i\leq M
, then

\mathbb{E}\|(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\|^{2}=\sum_{j=0}^{k}(\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj})+\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}],

and if
M<i\leq N
, then
\mathbb{E}\|(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\|^{2}=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}]\leq\sum_{j=0}^{k}(\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj})+\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}],
using our convention that
\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}=0
for
i>M
.
Define
\mathbb{E}_{k}:=\sum_{j=0}^{k}(\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj})+\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}]
. Using these facts,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left([T2]^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4}\right)&=\mathbb{P}\left(\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\phi\left(\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)-\phi\left(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i}\right)\right|\right]^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\|^{2}+\|[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}\|^{2}\right)\geq\frac{\min\{1,1/(8L^{2})\}\,(\epsilon^{2}/4)}{2+8\mathbb{E}_{k}}\right)\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\|^{2}\geq 2\mathbb{E}_{k}\right).\end{split}

(6.82)

Observe that the second term of (
6.82
) equals

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\|(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\|(\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i},\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i},[\mathbf{w}]_{i})\|^{2}\right)\geq\mathbb{E}_{k}\right).

(6.83)

Concentration for this follows directly by Lemma
17
since
(p_{1},p_{2},w)\mapsto\|(p_{1},p_{2},w)\|^{2}
is pseudo-Lipschitz.

To bound the first term of (
6.82
), observe that by Lemma
4
part 2,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left(\left[\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}-\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}_{j}^{\mathrm{in}}\right]_{i}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}]_{r}\left[\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\right]_{i}\right]^{2}.

Next, by Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}]_{r}^{2}\right]\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}\left(\left[\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\right]_{i}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}]_{r}^{2}\right]\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\|^{2}\right].

(6.84)

Observe that part 3 of Lemma
4
implies that
\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\mathbb{E}\left[([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i})^{2}\right]=\sum_{r=0}^{k}\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}^{2}
,where the second equality follows from part 3 of Lemma
4
as
([\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i})^{2}=(\sum_{r=0}^{k}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}\,[\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\,\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}]_{i})^{2}\overset{d}{=}(\sum_{r=0}^{k}\sqrt{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}}\,[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}\,[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}]_{i})^{2}
with
[\overline{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}]_{i}
being i.i.d. standard Gaussian; therefore,

\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]_{r}^{2}\leq\frac{\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}{\min_{0\leq i\leq k}(\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pi})}\leq\frac{\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}{\epsilon^{*}_{1}},

using the stopping criterion. Now using this bound in (
6.84
), we find

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}]_{r}^{2}\right]\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{k}[\mathbf{c}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}]_{r}^{2}\right]\left[\sum_{r=0}^{j}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}{\epsilon^{*}_{1}}\right]\sum_{j=0}^{k}\sum_{r=0}^{j}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\|^{2}.

Now, combining the above, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2}\geq\frac{\min\{1,1/(8L^{2})\}\,(\epsilon^{2}/4)}{2+8\sum_{j=0}^{k}(\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj})+8\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}]}\right)

(6.85)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\sum_{r=0}^{j}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\|^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon^{*}_{1}\min(1,\frac{1}{8L^{2}})\,(\epsilon^{2}/4)}{\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}(2+8\sum_{j=1}^{k}(\tau^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}+\tau^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj})+8\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}])}\right)

(6.86)

\displaystyle\leq k\sum_{r=0}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pr}\|^{2}}{N}\geq\frac{c\epsilon^{2}}{k^{3}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}Ck^{4}\exp\left(-c\epsilon^{2}/k^{7}\right),

(6.87)

where
c,C
are constants, and the inequality
(a)
follows from
\mathbf{P_{i}.(a)}
for
0\leq i\leq k
. Next, consider

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}-\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2}=\delta\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\|\left[\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}-\left[\underline{\accentset{*}{\mathbf{p}}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\right]_{i}\|^{2},

where
\delta=M/N
and the equality follows from the convention for
i>N
. Concentration for this term follows exactly the same as above, with the only difference coming from the appearance of a
\delta^{-1}
factor in the exponential rate.
Combining these with lemma
9
gives concentration for the first term above, which completes the proof of
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
.

\mathbf{P_{k}.(c)}

Observe that, by Lemma
8
,

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{1}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})=[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i}\in PL(2).

Thus, by
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
, we have that
\frac{1}{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}[P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]=0
(as
W^{p}
is assumed independent of
(P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{0},\ldots,P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k})
), as needed.

\mathbf{P_{k}.(d)}

Again by Lemma
8
, for all
0\leq j\leq k
,

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{1}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})=[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}\in PL(2).

By
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
, we find
\frac{1}{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}[P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{j}P^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}_{k}]
, as needed.

\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
We note here that we prove
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
and then prove
\mathbf{P_{k}.(e)}
afterwards, as the proof of
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
relies on the result of
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
.

Concentration for
\alpha
terms.

First, we show
\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
. To this end, recall that

\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\mathcal{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}),

where
\mathcal{T}
is the truncation operator defined as
\mathcal{T}x=\min(t_{2},\max(t_{1},x))
. Recall that the upper truncation is only necessary if
\log p(x)
or
\log p(y\mid z)
is not strongly concave. Now, adopting the notation
[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}=\left([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i}\right)
and
\gamma_{pk}=(\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})
, observe that by Lemma
9
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\mathcal{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}\right)-\mathcal{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

(6.88)

Label the two terms of (
6.88
) as
T1
and
T2
.

For term
T2
of (
6.88
), because
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\in[t_{\min},t_{\max}]
(by
Condition 6
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

By the assumption that
[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}
is assumed either uniformly Lipschitz or bounded conditionally Lipschitz in
Condition 3
, we have concentration for the right side by
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
.

For term
T1
of (
6.88
), observe that for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,
|\mathcal{T}x-\mathcal{T}y|\leq|x-y|
; therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\mathcal{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}\right)-\mathcal{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}\right)-[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big).

(6.89)

Now, by
Condition 3
, the functions
[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}
are uniformly Lipschitz around
\overline{\gamma}_{pk}
. This guarantees that there exists some
\delta>0
such that if
\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|<\delta
and
\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|<\delta
, then

\displaystyle\left|[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}\right)-[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\leq L_{p}\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\right].

In particular, this implies that if
\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|<\delta
and
\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|<\delta
, and if

\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\right]\frac{L_{p}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2},

then

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}\right)-[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk}\right)\Big|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

It then follows from (
6.89
) that
T1
can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\right]\frac{L_{p}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

(6.90)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right).

The second and third terms of (
6.90
) concentrate by
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
, and the first term concentrates using Lemma
11
along with
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
and
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
, because
1+\|([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|
is pseudo-Lipschitz of order
2
and
\mathbb{E}\|(P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}},P_{k}^{\mathrm{out}},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})\|
is upper bounded by a universal constant.

Concentration for
\gamma
terms.

We now show concentration of
\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
around
\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}
. We can assume that the clipping of the
\gamma
iterates is absorbed by the
\Gamma
functions, because clipping a Lipschitz function to bound its range within a compact interval gives another Lipschitz function with the same constant
L_{1}
. Observe that by (
8
) in Algorithm
3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{qk}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}\left(\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)-\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}\left(\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|+\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{L_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2L_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2L_{1}}\right).

(6.91)

where
(a)
follows from the assumption that
\Gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}
is Lipschitz continuous on its domain along with the fact that
\|(\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})\|\leq|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}|+|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}|
and
(b)
by Lemma
9
. The final terms in the above can be upper bounded by the work just above in (
6.88
)-(
6.90
) and
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
.

Concentration for inner product terms.

We show concentration for
[\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}
where
0\leq j\leq k
. Recall that by (
6
) in Algorithm
3
,
[\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}=(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}),
where we have defined functions

\displaystyle g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}):=\mathbf{f}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}})-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},

(6.92)

where to simplify notation, we write
[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}=\left([\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}_{k}^{\mathrm{out}}]_{i}\right)
and
\gamma_{pk}=\left(\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{in}},\gamma_{pk}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)
.
Observe that

[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})=(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}\left[[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\right].

Now by Lemma
11
it suffices to show concentration separately for
(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}
and

[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}.

For
(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}
, we have,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}-(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{L_{2}}\right),

(6.93)

where the inequality follows from the fact that
\frac{1}{1-a}
is Lipschitz on
[t_{1},t_{2}]
with Lipschitz constant
L_{2}:=(1-t_{2})^{-2}
and the fact that the
\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
lie in
[t_{1},t_{2}]
by
Assumption 4
. The right hand side then concentrates by the work for
\alpha
concentration above (
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
; the first part).

Next, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{N}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\right]\right|\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\left(f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\right)\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right),

(6.94)

where
(a)
follows by adding and subtracting
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}
from each term and applying the triangle inequality. Now the first term in (
6.3
) concentrates by Lemma
11
,
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
(the first part), and
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
since
[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\in PL(2)
.

Adding and subtracting
[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\left(f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\right)
, we bound the second term in (
6.3
) by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\left(f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\right)\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)

(6.95)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})-f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right).

The second of these terms can be bounded in exactly the same way as
T1b
in
\mathbf{P_{k}.(e)}
below.

For the first term in (
6.95
), note that if

f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})
is Lipschitz (in all inputs), then

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})=[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\left(f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}\right),

is in
PL(2)
by Lemma
8
. If instead
f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})
satisfies the bounded conditionally Lipschitz condition, then
\phi
is bounded conditionally pseudo-Lipschitz. In either case,
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
tells us that this will concentrate around

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{P^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}\left(f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right)\right\}.

(6.96)

Therefore, to complete the proof, we just need to show that the expression in (
6.96
) is equal to zero. To do this, recall that
\mathbb{E}[P_{j}^{\mathrm{in}}]=0
, so (
6.96
) is equal to

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}

\displaystyle\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\mathbb{E}\left\{P^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right\}\times\mathbb{E}\left\{[f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]^{\prime}(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right\}=0,

where the first equality follows from Stein’s lemma, and the second equality follows from the definition of
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
in the GVAMP state evolution (
4.36
).

Concentration for
[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}
is proved in the same way as the above.

\mathbf{P_{k}.(e)}

By (
6
) in Algorithm
3
and the definition of the function
g_{p}
in (
6.92
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}[\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-\left[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\right]_{(j+1)(k+1)}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)

(6.97)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj})}{(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj})}-\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}V^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]\right|\geq\epsilon\right).

Using Lemma
10
, we can separately show concentration for
(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj})^{-1}
and

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}).

First observe that the function
f(a)=\frac{1}{1-a}
is Lipschitz over the interval
[t_{1},t_{2}]
within which the
\alpha
iterates must lie given our truncation assumption (
Assumption 4
) and we can take
L_{2}=\frac{1}{(1-t_{2})^{2}}
to be the Lipschitz constant. Therefore,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}-\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{L_{2}}\right).

(6.98)

The right side of (
6.98
) concentrates by
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
. Now
\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}}
concentrates in the same way using
\mathbf{P_{j}.(f)}
, and concentration for
(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}(1-\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj})^{-1}
follows by Lemma
10
.

Next,
using Lemma
9
, we have that

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}&\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})-\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right)\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}V^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big)\\
&+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})-\left[1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right]\left[1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right]\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}V^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).\end{split}

(6.99)

We label these latter two terms
T1
and
T2
and analyze them separately.

Concentration for T1 of (
6.99
).

Observe that repeated application of the triangle inequality the definition of the function
g_{p}
in (
6.92
) gives us

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big[g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})-g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\frac{1}{N}\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right|\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\right|+\frac{1}{N}\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\frac{1}{N}\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right|\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})\right|.

By Lemmas
9
and
11
, to establish concentration for these terms, it suffices to show concentration for each of the following:

\displaystyle\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|,

\displaystyle\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right|,

\displaystyle\left|\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right|,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f_{p}^{k}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk}).

Concentration for the first two follows by
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
and
\mathbf{P_{j}.(f)}
above, and concentration for the third follows from that and Lemma
10
. Concentration for fourth term follows from
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
as

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})=[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i},

is pseudo-Lipschitz of order
2
by Lemma
8
.

Thus, it remains to show concentration for the last two terms above.
For simplicity, we show this only for the first of these, with the other following in the same way. First, defining

\mu:=\mathbb{E}\left\{P^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}f_{p}\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)\right\}

and writing
\overline{\gamma}_{pk}=\left(\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right)
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}&\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-\mu\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})-\mu\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}\left(f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})\right)\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).\end{split}

(6.100)

Let the terms on the right hand side of (
6.100
) be
T1a
and
T1b
, respectively. First we analyze
T1a
. By assumption,
f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w,\gamma^{\mathrm{in}},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}})
is either uniformly Lipschitz or uniformly bounded conditionally Lipschitz (UBCL) at
(\gamma^{\mathrm{in}},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}})=(\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})
. By definition, this implies eithery way that
f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w,\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})
is either Lipschitz in
(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w)
or bounded conditionally Lipschitz in
(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}})
for each
w^{p}
. Thus, the function

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})=[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}f_{p}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})

is either pseudo-Lipschitz of order
2
or bounded conditionally pseudo-Lipschitz by Lemma
8
. Therefore, concentration for
T1a
follows from
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
.

Now for
T1b
, we have by the definition of uniform Lipschitz continuity and UBCL that there is some
\delta>0
such that
\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|<\delta
and
\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|<\delta
implies that

\displaystyle\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w,\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})-f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w,\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq L_{p}\left(1+\|(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w)\|\right)\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\right].

Following the work in (
6.90
)- (
6.89
), we have the upper bound

\displaystyle T_{1}b

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)

(6.101)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(L_{p}\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\right]\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}|\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\right]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

The first two terms above concentrates by
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
, and the third concentrates using Lemma
11
combined with
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
and
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
since

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})=|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}|\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)

is pseudo-Lipschitz of order
2
by Lemma
8
. This establishes concentration for
T1b
.

Concentration for T2 of (
6.99
).

We have to show concentration for

T2=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})-\left[1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right]\left[1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right]\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}V^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

(6.102)

Adding and subtracting
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})
, we find that from Lemma
9
,

\begin{split}&T2\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})-\left[1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right]\left[1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right]\mathbb{E}[V^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}V^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\\
&+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})\right.\right.\right.\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\left.\left.\left.g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})\right]\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).\end{split}

(6.103)

By the same reasoning used above,
g_{p}(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w,\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})
is Lipschitz in
(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}},w)
or UBCL in
(p^{\mathrm{in}},p^{\mathrm{out}})
for each
w
. Therefore, using Lemma
8
and
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
, the function in the first term on the right side of (
6.103
) is either pseudo-Lipschtz or bounded conditionally pseudo-Lipschitz, and thus the first term in (
6.103
) concentrates
because

\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)\left(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right)\mathbb{E}\{V^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}V^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}\}=\mathbb{E}\left\{g_{p}\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{k},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)g_{p}\left(P^{\mathrm{in}}_{j},P^{\mathrm{out}}_{j},W^{\mathrm{in}}_{p},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj},\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right)\right\}.

Thus, we just need to show that the second term on the right side of (
6.103
) concentrates.
Applying the definition of the function
g_{p}
in (
6.92
) and expanding terms, we see that

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})-g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})g_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj},\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})\right]\right|\\
&\leq\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i}|\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj})-f_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj})\right|\\
&\quad+\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}]_{i}|\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-f_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})\right|\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj})-f_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj})\Big|.\end{split}

(6.104)

Notice that the first two terms on the right side of (
6.104
) concentrate in the same way as term T1b of (
6.100
). Namely, using the uniformly Lipschitz or UBCL property of
f_{p}^{\mathrm{in}}
, we can bound the sums by sums of (bounded conditionally) pseudo-Lipschitz functions.
Considering the third term on the right side of (
6.104
), we apply Lemma
9
and then to complete the proof, we obtain concentration for the following three quantities:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})\right|\Big|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj})-f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj})\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj})\right|\Big|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pk})-f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})\Big|\Big|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\gamma_{pj})-f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pj})\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big).

Let these probabilities be labeled as
T2a-T2c
.

Following the work in (
6.90
)- (
6.89
) and using the uniformly (bounded conditionally) Lipschitz property of
f_{p}
, we have the upper bound

\displaystyle T_{2}a

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)

(6.105)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj}\right|\right]\frac{L_{1}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[w^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})\right|\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\geq\epsilon\right).

The first two terms can be bounded using
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
. The last term, in turn, can be upper bounded using Lemma
11
along with
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
and
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
since by Lemma
8

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{p}]_{i})=\left|f^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i},\overline{\gamma}_{pk})\right|\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)

is (bounded conditionally) pseudo-Lipschitz of order
2
. Term
T2b
can be bounded in exactly the same way as
T2a
.

For
T_{2}c
, we again use the strategy as in (
6.90
)- (
6.89
) to provide the following upper bound for term
T_{2}c
. We have

\displaystyle T_{2}c

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj}\right|\geq\min\{1,\delta\}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\Big(\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pj}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pj}\right|\right]\left[\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|+\left|\gamma^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}-\overline{\gamma}^{\mathrm{out}}_{pk}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big).

(6.106)

We know from
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
and
\mathbf{Q_{j}.(f)}
that the first four terms concentrate as needed. Then the last term in (
6.3
) can be upper bounded with repeated application of Lemmas
11
and
9
along with
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(f)}
,
\mathbf{Q_{j}.(f)}
,
\mathbf{P_{k}.(b)}
, and the fact that

\phi([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}]_{i},\ldots,[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})=\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)\left(1+\|([\mathbf{p}_{j}]_{i},[\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}]_{i})\|\right)

is pseudo-Lipschitz of order
2
by Lemma
8
.

\mathbf{P_{k}.(g).(i)}

We want to show that
\frac{1}{N}\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}
concentrates around
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
. To this end, observe

\displaystyle\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}[\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}

\displaystyle=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k},

(6.107)

where the second equality follows because
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
along with the fact that
\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}[\mathbf{B}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}
and
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}
are both the orthogonal projection onto
\mathrm{range}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\right)
.

We know that
\frac{1}{N}[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
concentrates around
\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\right)_{k+1,k+1}
by
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(e)}
. Next observe that with
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}:=([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}
, from the definition of
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}
in (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{i,j=0}^{k-1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i})([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j})[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i+1,j+1}+\sum_{i,j=0}^{k-1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i})([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j})[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i+1+k,j+1+k}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\sum_{i,j=0}^{k-1}([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i})([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j})[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i+1+k,j+1}.

(6.108)

Let these terms be labeled
T_{1}-T_{3}
. For
T_{1}
, observe that because
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
,

([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i})([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j})=([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i})([\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}),

(6.109)

which concentrates around
[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]_{i+1,k+1}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]_{j+1,k+1}=\mathbb{E}\left[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}U^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[U^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}U^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\right]=[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]_{i+1}[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]_{j+1}
by
\mathbf{Q_{k}.(e)}
and Lemma
10
, where the equality follows from (
4.39
) and (
4.40
). Furthermore, by
\mathbf{P_{k-1}.(g).(iv)}
,
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i,j}=[([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}]_{i,j}
concentrates around
[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k-1)}]_{ij}=[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)})^{-1}]_{i,j}
(by (
4.42
)).
Thus, again using Lemma
10
and Lemma
9
, we get that
T_{1}
concentrates around

\sum_{i,j=0}^{k-1}[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]_{i+1}[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]_{j+1}[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)})^{-1}]_{i+1,j+1}=[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]^{T}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)}]^{-1}\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}.

For
T_{2}
, we proceed similarly, noting that
([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i})([\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{in}}_{j})
concentrates around
0
by
\mathbf{P_{k}.(f)}
and Lemma
10
. Because
[\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]_{i+1+k,j+1+k}
concentrates around
[(\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k-1)})^{-1}]_{i,j}
by
\mathbf{P_{k-1}.(g).(iv)}
, by Lemma
11
and
9
we find that
T_{2}
concentrates around
0
. By analogous reasoning,
T_{3}
also concentrates around
0
.

Thus, combining the above with Lemma
9
and using (
4.41
), we find
\|\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}
concentrates on

\left[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\right]_{k+1,k+1}-[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]^{T}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)}]^{-1}[\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]=\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}.

\mathbf{P_{k}.(g).(ii)}

First we establish that
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is invertible with high probability. Before we can do this, we make a minor technical adjustment to simplify notation. First recall from (
4.2
) that
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=[\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\;\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k-1}]
. Next, define the
(2k-1)\times(2k-1)
matrix

\mathbf{F}=\left[\mathbf{e}_{k}\;\mathbf{e}_{1}\;\mathbf{e}_{2}\;\cdots\;\mathbf{e}_{k-1}\;\mathbf{e}_{k+1}\;\cdots\;\mathbf{e}_{2k-1}\right].

Observe that multiplying a matrix on the right by
\mathbf{F}
has the effect of making the
k^{\mathrm{th}}
column the first column. Also observe that
\mathbf{F}
has an inverse, we call this
\mathbf{G}
and note that
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{G}\mathbf{F}=\mathbb{I}
. Now with this notation, let

\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}:=\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\mathbf{F}=\left[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\;\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\right]
where
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}=[\mathbf{P}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k-1}\;\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k-1}].

Furthermore, we see

\mathbf{G}^{T}\left([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{G}^{T}\left([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\mathbf{F}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\mathbf{F}\right)\mathbf{G}=[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk},

(6.110)

which implies that if
[\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is invertible, then so is
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
. Symmetrically, we see that
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is invertible if and only if
[\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is invertible. Moreover, we have from (
6.110
) that when
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
is invertible,

([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}=[\mathbf{G}^{T}\left([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)\mathbf{G}]^{-1}=\mathbf{F}([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}\mathbf{F}^{T}.

Now, the right hand side of the above differs from
([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}
by rearranging the columns and rows. In particular, the right hand side has the same entries as
([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}
up to rearrangement. Therefore it suffices to show that the entries of
([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}
concentrate.

To this end, we observe that

[\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}=\left[\begin{matrix}[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}&[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\\
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}&[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\end{matrix}\right].

(6.111)

Now, by (
6.3
) in
\mathbf{P_{k}.(g).(i)}
, we have that

\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}=[\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}-[\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}.

Define
\bm{\nu}=([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
. Using (
6.111
), the block matrix inversion formula gives us that, if
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}
is invertible and
\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|\neq 0
, then

\left([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right)^{-1}=\left[\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk})^{-1}\end{matrix}\right]+\frac{1}{\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}}\left[\begin{matrix}1&-\bm{\nu}^{T}\\
-\bm{\nu}&\bm{\nu}\bm{\nu}^{T}\end{matrix}\right].

(6.112)

Now, by
\mathbf{P_{k}.(g).(i)}
, we know that
\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}
concentrates around
\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}>\epsilon^{*}_{1}>0
(where the first inequality is due to the stopping criterion). Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\text{ singular}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left([\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\text{ singular}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\text{ singular}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\|[\mathbf{B}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{C}_{vk}^{\mathrm{in}}}]^{T}\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\|^{2}-\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon^{*}_{1}}{2}\right).

(6.113)

The first term concentrates by
\mathbf{P_{k-1}.(g).(iv)}
, and the second concentrates by
\mathbf{P_{k}.(g).(i)}
.

Next, we show entrywise concentration of
([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}
around
\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot}}{{=}}\left[\begin{matrix}[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}]^{-1}&0\\
0&[\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k-1)}]^{-1}\end{matrix}\right]
, where the definition is originally given in (
4.42
). First, define two events
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{k}:=\left\{[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\text{ singular}\right\}
and
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{k-1}:=\left\{[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{vk}\text{ singular}\right\}
. Then, notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left[([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}\right]_{ij}-\left[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\right]_{ij}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{k})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}^{2}_{k-1})+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left[([\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{-1}\right]_{ij}-\left[\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\right]_{ij}\right|\geq\epsilon\;\Bigg|\;(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{k})^{c}\cap(\mathcal{F}^{2}_{k-1})^{c}\right),

(6.114)

where
(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{k})^{c}
and
(\mathcal{F}^{2}_{k-1})^{c}
denote the complimentary events. We mention that the first two terms on the right side of (
6.3
) concentrate by (
6.3
) and
\mathbf{P_{k-1}.(g).(iv)}
. Moving forward, we focus on the third term on the right side of (
6.3
).

As noted above, we can reorder the columns, so it suffices to replace
[\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
by
[\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}]^{T}\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
and
\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}
by
\mathbf{F}\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\mathbf{F}^{T}
. Notice that
\mathbf{F}A^{-1}\mathbf{F}^{T}=(\mathbf{F}A\mathbf{F}^{T})^{-1}
. Then, by the block matrix inversion formula and the fact that
\mathbb{E}\left[P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[U_{k}^{\mathrm{in}/\mathrm{out}}\right]^{2}
we find

\begin{split}\mathbf{F}\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}\mathbf{F}^{T}=\left(\mathbf{F}\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{uk}&0\\
0&\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k-1)}\end{matrix}\right]\mathbf{F}^{T}\right)^{-1}&=\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbb{E}\{[P_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}]^{2}\}&\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}&0\\
(\mathbf{b}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk})^{T}&\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{u(k-1)}&0\\
0&0&\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k-1)}\end{matrix}\right]^{-1}\\
&=\left[\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&\bm{\Pi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{v(k-1)}\end{matrix}\right]+\frac{1}{\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}}\left[\begin{matrix}1&-[\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\;0]\\
-\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\mathrm{in}}_{pk}\\
